# Jozsef Moldovan Katz parentage research

**Current research scope, 19 October 2026:** Hillel stopped the MyHeritage subscription. No further MyHeritage queries
or paid-record access are authorized. Continue the 149-person tree investigation primarily through the existing
authenticated Geni session, in depth-first order, inspecting accessible profiles, sources, media and revisions.
Earlier MyHeritage captures remain historical evidence; pending MyHeritage queries are suspended, not completed. The
already-read Yad Vashem originals are recorded separately below. Private fields remain unavailable. Initial Geni
child-profile reads are prefetch until evaluated in order; no claim of a completed all-person DFS is made.

**Current publication instruction, 19 October 2026:** GitHub/GitHub Pages only. The Stash artifact was removed at Hillel’s request; all 17 files were verified preserved in live GitHub. Earlier Stash publication statements below are dated history; the artifact will not be recreated.

**Accuracy update, 18 October 2026:** the [140-profile evidence audit](#accuracy-review-18-october-2026) supersedes earlier conflicting wording below. Source review is not verification of every imported claim.

Identify the father and mother of **Burek Jozsef Moldovan Katz**, also recorded as Joszep, Joszip, Joszif and under Moldovan, Katz and Kahan. His WikiTree birth around 1790 and death around 1837 are estimates. **Neither parent is identified by the evidence examined so far.** Updated 13 October 2026.

## Possible relatives and parentage leads

| Person | Evidence | Possible relationship to Jozsef | What would establish the link |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Lazar Moldovan of Alsó Rhona** | 1795 census: wife, one son, two daughters; family members unnamed. | Older-generation lead; fatherhood unproved. | Identify the son and trace this household into later records. |
| **Slom Moldovan of Bréb** | 1821–1822: wife, three daughters, one sibling. Original 1848 census: age 60, born in Moldavia, resident in Hungary and Bréb for 55 years. | Possible sibling or other relative. His recorded age places him near Jozsef’s estimated generation. | Check the original sibling column and remarks; identify the sibling through additional records if only a count survives. |
| **Slyim or Sloim Moldovan, died 1864** | Death indexes give age 105; one transcription dates death 14 October, another 12 October. | Potential older-generation relative, or the same Slom with inconsistent age information. | Read the original death entry and resolve identity before using the age. |
| **Simon Katz of Brébb** | Listed alongside Joszep Katz in the 1813–1814 and 1814–1815 tax lists. | Possible relative; no relationship stated. | Trace Simon separately and compare original names and households. |
| **Hers Moldovan of Bréb** | Original 1828 property census inspected, entry 4; births from 1824 onward name Hers and Leje or Laje. | Another local family branch to distinguish. | Find a record naming his parents or explicitly relating him to Jozsef. |

## Original census evidence

**Slom Moldovan, Bréb, 1848:** household 1 records age **60**, country of birth **Moldavia**, an unknown birthplace town, and **55 years** of residence both in Hungary and in Bréb. Arrival around **1793**, at roughly five years old, is an inference from those two recorded figures. His wife Toba is 35, from Barczanfalva; children include Cill, Eszter Malka, Golda, Liber and Volf. The census records a Moldavian birthplace for Slom; Jozsef’s birthplace remains unestablished.

[Original image 17, first household](https://www.familysearch.org/ark:/61903/3:1:3Q9M-CS84-X9QJ-8). Film 719825, image group 007953483.

**Leb Moldovan, Gyulafalva, 1848:** household 6 records age 22, born in Bréb, a wife aged 22 and son Mojse aged one. This supports the branch identified on WikiTree as Jozsef and Rifke’s son Chaim Arye Leb.

[Original image 9, first household on the page](https://www.familysearch.org/ark:/61903/3:1:3Q9M-CS84-X9QK-Y).

## Jozsef’s household

The profile’s citations place Joszep Katz in Brébb in 1813–1814 and Joszip Moldovan there in 1821–1822 with a wife, three sons and one daughter. Birth records name wife Tsarna in 1824 and 1826. Tsarna died on 21 February 1826, aged 38. Subsequent births name Rifke, including Hajim on 24 June 1827; later entries use Ilona or Ilona/Rifke. A 1838 tax entry describes the widow of Joszip Katz. These records establish family context but do not name Jozsef’s parents.

[WikiTree profile and citations](https://www.wikitree.com/wiki/Moldovan_Katz-1).

## Records to obtain next

1. **Lazar Moldovan, 1795:** Alsó Rhona, Szigeth district, Máramaros; pages 102–103, entry 4. Index reference: **MOL, DJ(C55), 1795, No. 1? or 13?** The uncertainty in the reference must be preserved.

2. **Slom and Joszip Moldovan, 1821–1822:** Brébb, Kaszo district; pages 317–318. Reference: **MOL, DJ(C55), 1822, F1, No. 144**. Slom’s unnamed sibling is the central lead.

3. **Simon and Joszep Katz, 1813–1814:** Brébb, tax list page 54, entries 3 and 5; 1814–1815 page 60, entries 2 and 3. Hungarian National Archives, Máramaros tolerance-tax lists.

4. **Slyim/Sloim’s 1864 death:** Bréb Register 1271, page-row **05-04**; parallel index references Fund 1408, Book 342, **53-4** and **54-1**. Verify date, age and any identifying text.

[JewishGen census collection](https://www.jewishgen.org/databases/Hungary/CensusOther.htm), [Máramaros register inventory](https://www.jewishgen.org/databases/Hungary/Maramaros.htm).

## Gaps and identity conflicts

The indexed death entries from Bréb Register 1271 jump from 1826 to 1863. Jozsef’s estimated death falls within that gap. This describes the index coverage; it does not establish that the original register lacks the record.

Slom’s original census age of 60 in 1848 conflicts with the 1864 death index age of 105. Keep the identities unresolved. Similar surnames, neighboring households and repeated given names alone do not establish parentage.

A separate death record for **Abraham Moldovan**, aged 85, on **10 December 1896**, names parents **Lazar Moldovan and Czarna Gancz** and wife Nyicza Anna Markovics. It identifies a real couple for further comparison, but neither their equivalence to the 1795 Lazar household nor their relationship to Jozsef is established. The original has now been inspected: official catalog **MM-F-00743-1-1-00288**, page **60**, entry **89**. The earlier index-derived reference used Fund 753; see the original-record details below.

[JewishGen Hungary database](https://www.jewishgen.org/databases/Hungary/).

## Research status

Authenticated JewishGen searches examined the early Bréb census entries, surname-wide Moldovan census matches, relevant Bréb birth and death entries, and Alsó Rhona’s early census material. Original 1848 images for Bréb and Gyulafalva were inspected. The original 1795 and 1821–1822 documents and the 1864 death entry remain to be located.

## Further findings from this research pass

**Original 1828 entries located and inspected:** Bréb’s register lists **Hers Moldovan at entry 4**, **Jozip Moldovan at entry 87**, and **Schlom Moldovan at entry 104**. Jozip and Schlom carry a Jewish designation in the handwriting. The entries are separate and do not state kinship. Column 2 counts taxable persons aged 18–60, so its figure of two must not be treated as the complete family size.

[Hers, image 2](https://adatbazisokonline.mnl.gov.hu/hwrview/1828/C52BA9B48E414C8C95D02B861B645720/), [Jozip, image 10](https://adatbazisokonline.mnl.gov.hu/hwrview/1828/96163AF1F24C45FF55277E4C3C3213EB/), [Schlom, image 12](https://adatbazisokonline.mnl.gov.hu/hwrview/1828/0DEC4AD5E30C4CB0155842FEB4BFA0AF/). Official archive collection: HU-MNL-OL-W-21-6-N-26, Máramaros, Bréb (18).

**Qualification on the 1795 and 1821–1822 leads:** the archive’s inventory describes the standard tolerance-tax form as naming the household head and counting wives, children, relatives and siblings. Therefore an original may confirm the sibling column without naming Slom’s sibling. Originals are still worth checking for remarks, but a numerical sibling count alone cannot identify Jozsef.

[Hungarian National Archives inventory, printed pages 182–185](https://real-eod.mtak.hu/16492/1/MolDigiLib_MOLkiadv1_03.pdf).

**Access checks:** an authenticated FamilySearch catalog search for “Judaeorum” returned the 1725–1775 Acta Judaeorum collection and the 1848 census. Those date ranges do not cover the target 1795 and 1822 records. The archive’s Electronic Archives portal failed to load in both the web reader and connected browser. This is an access failure, not evidence that C 55 is undigitized or that the documents are absent.

[FamilySearch catalog query](https://www.familysearch.org/en/search/catalog/results?q.keywords=Judaeorum), [official archive finding-aid routes](https://mnl.gov.hu/angol/mnl/ol/databases_finding_aids?theme=mnlvakbarat).

**Original death image route:** JewishGen’s Sub-Carpathia collection provides an [original-image request link](https://tinyurl.com/SubcarpathiaDocRequest). The request form’s contents were not verified and nothing was submitted. For the Romanian Bréb Register 1271, the [Máramaros project inventory](https://www.jewishgen.org/databases/Hungary/Maramaros.htm) confirms the book and identifies Baia Mare as the repository.

## Exact follow-up requests

- **Hungarian National Archives / census indexing project:** obtain C 55, 1822, F1, No. 144, Brébb, Kaszo district, pages 317–318, including column headings and margins; inspect Joszip and Slom Moldovan. Also resolve the uncertain 1795 reference and obtain Alsó Rhona pages 102–103, entry 4, Lazar Moldovan.

- **Máramaros / Sub-Carpathia register projects:** obtain Bréb Register 1271, death page-row 05-04, and Fund 1408, Book 342, pages 53–54. Compare Slyim/Sloim/Sljom’s dates of 12 and 14 October 1864 and recorded age 105 against Slom’s 1848 age 60.

- **Collateral parentage lead — original obtained:** Abraham Moldovan’s 10 December 1896 death entry was inspected. It names Lazar Moldovan and Czarna Gancz as his parents. Seek an earlier record corroborating their parentage and a separate record connecting Abraham to Jozsef.

These are prepared research requests, not messages sent to an archive or researcher.

## Gravestones and original records: additional findings, 13 October 2026

**Jozsef’s father and mother remain unidentified.** This pass recovered cemetery photographs, checked burial records,
and inspected an original Romanian-held death register. It establishes the parent names recorded for Abraham
Moldovan, a possible collateral relative, but does not establish that Abraham and Jozsef were related.

### Original record now inspected: Abraham Moldovan

The original Hungarian-language civil death entry identifies **Moldován Ábrahám**, an Israelite day laborer, born and
resident in Bréb, aged 85, who died there on **10 December 1896**. Registration was on 11 December. His wife is recorded
as Markovics Nyicza Anna. The parent fields read:

- Father: **“néhai Moldován Lázár”** — the late Lazar Moldovan; day laborer; Bréb.
- Mother: **“néhai Gancz Czárna”** — the late Czarna Gancz; household occupation; Bréb.

There is high confidence that these names are what the original record states. As information supplied at an adult’s
death, the parentage still merits corroboration in an earlier family record. The recorded age implies birth around
1811, not an independently established birth date.

**Source:** Romanian National Archives, Maramureș, **MM-F-00743-1-1-00288**, Ocna Șugatag deaths 1895–1899,
folio/page **60**, entry **89**. PDF **MM-FD-00743-1-1-0288_040.pdf**, PDF page **22**, right-hand entry.
The current official catalog uses **00743**; the earlier JewishGen-derived citation in our ledger used **753**.
Use the official reference when requesting this scanned volume.

[Official catalog and downloads](https://descopera.arhivelenationale.ro/cota/?cid=13914568) ·
[Original PDF, page 22](https://descopera.arhivelenationale.ro/api/media/file?id=13914568&type=fisiere&mediaId=486886#page=22)

**Implication for Jozsef:** this places a documented Lazar–Czarna couple in the Bréb family context. It does not show
that this Lazar is the 1795 Alsó Rhona household head, or that either parent was Jozsef’s parent. No new parent should
be added to Jozsef’s tree on this evidence.

### Gravestone search: an explicit father name, but no identification with Jozsef

A photographed stone in **Seini**, indexed as Iosef Katz, contains **“יוסף בר יקותיאל כ״ץ”**: Yosef, son of Yekutiel,
Katz. The photographed death line and JOWBR identify **16 Adar 5600 (1840)**. The stone has painted number **123**.
The image was inspected directly, not merely inferred from the index.

[Burial record J_MARAMURE_0009030](https://www.jewishgen.org/databases/cemetery/jowbr.php?rec=J_MARAMURE_0009030) ·
[Gravestone photograph](https://data.jewishgen.org/imagedata/jowbr/ROM-01877/P9050162.JPG)

**Yekutiel is not an established father of our Jozsef.** The burial is in Seini rather than Bréb, and the 1840 death
conflicts with the existing 1838 index entry describing Jozsef Katz’s widow. The underlying 1838 tax list has not yet
been inspected, so the conflict needs original-source checking; it cannot be silently discarded. There is no spouse,
child, residence history, or other connecting evidence tying this stone to Burek Jozsef Moldovan Katz.

Two other superficially attractive burial matches were checked and rejected as identifications:

- [Borech Moldovan / Baruch, Satu Mare](https://www.jewishgen.org/databases/cemetery/jowbr.php?rec=J_MARAMURE_0003659):
  Hebrew death date 3 Nissan 5701 (1941), incompatible with this early nineteenth-century family chronology.
- [József Kahan / Yosef Baruch, Satu Mare](https://www.jewishgen.org/databases/cemetery/jowbr.php?rec=J_MARAMURE_0002895):
  Hebrew death date 13 Kislev 5700 (1939), likewise incompatible.

### Bréb cemetery photographs recovered from an expired website

The [JewishGen cemetery survey](https://cdp.jewishgen.org/eastern-europe/romania/breb-maramure-judet) links Charles
Burns’s 2018 Bréb photographs. The live Galician Traces link redirected to unrelated advertising during this pass.
The Internet Archive preserved the [gallery page](https://web.archive.org/web/20190714054024/http://galiciantraces.com/breb-romania/).

Four images were recovered and inspected:

- [IMG_2581, full photograph](https://web.archive.org/web/20191015044952id_/http://galiciantraces.com/wp-content/gallery/breb/IMG_2581.JPG)
  — the prominent name reads Mordechai; no identification with Jozsef.
- [IMG_2576, thumbnail](https://web.archive.org/web/20191012180924id_/http://galiciantraces.com/wp-content/gallery/breb/thumbs/thumbs_IMG_2576.JPG).
- [IMG_2582, thumbnail](https://web.archive.org/web/20191012185725id_/http://galiciantraces.com/wp-content/gallery/breb/thumbs/thumbs_IMG_2582.JPG).
- [IMG_2584, thumbnail](https://web.archive.org/web/20191012195920id_/http://galiciantraces.com/wp-content/gallery/breb/thumbs/thumbs_IMG_2584.JPG).

The thumbnail inscriptions do not support a reliable identification of Jozsef or his parents. Four photographs are
not a complete cemetery survey. The historical survey describes extensive overgrowth and fallen stones; lack of an
online identification does not demonstrate that his grave is missing. Photo credit: Charles Burns / Galician Traces.

### Search coverage and limits

| Source and actual query | Result | What it establishes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Authenticated JOWBR, town exactly Bréb | 0 matches | No indexed match under that town query. |
| JOWBR full Romania cemetery inventory, including hidden town lists | Bréb not listed; Giulești has 11 entries | Bréb is a coverage gap in this inventory, not proof of no cemetery. |
| JOWBR Maramureș, surname phonetically like Moldovan | All 8 result rows inspected; Borech full record checked | No identified Jozsef; similar names are insufficient. |
| JOWBR Maramureș, given name Josef using site’s DM soundex, AND surname phonetically like Katz | All 17 full records inspected | Includes the 1840 Seini stone, undated entries, later people and matches through relatives’ names. |
| Same given-name query AND surname phonetically like Kahan | All 5 full records inspected | No identified target; Yosef Baruch died in 1939. |
| JOWBR, town exactly Giulești | All 11 result rows inspected | Includes Dov son of Chaim Aryeh HaKohen, a possible descendant-line lead; no proof of Jozsef’s parents. |
| Romanian archive text search Breb | All 206 result listings examined across three pages | Located civil registers and other holdings; no Bréb Jewish Book 1271 surfaced in these listings. This is not proof that the archive lacks it. |
| Official Ocna Șugatag death register 288 | Original entry 89 inspected | Confirms the parent names recorded for Abraham. |
| Hungarian C 55 digitization route | Found an official 2013 plan mentioning C 55 digitization | A plan is not evidence that the needed 1795/1822 scans are currently online. Those originals remain uninspected. |

[JOWBR inventory](https://www.jewishgen.org/databases/Cemetery/tree/CemList.php) ·
[Romanian archive query](https://descopera.arhivelenationale.ro/cautare-text/?ts=Breb&pg=1&pgs=100&cs=3&srt=0&sro=1) ·

### Highest-value next acquisitions

1. **The full surviving Bréb Jewish Register 1271**, including headings, margins and discontinuities. The project
   inventory lists births/deaths 1823–1883 and 77 indexed records. Obtain death pages around Tsarna’s 1826 entry and
   the transition to 1863; establish whether 1835–1838 deaths are absent from the original or only from the index.
   Also inspect birth entries 02-09 and 02-11 to resolve exactly how “Burek / Jozip” was written. The index’s slash
   should not be treated as a verified Hebrew double name or patronymic without the original.
2. **Original 1838 widow tax entry**, with its heading and neighboring household rows. This is necessary to test the
   apparent contradiction with the Seini stone, not a reason to assume the two men are identical.
3. **C 55, 1822, F1, No. 144, pages 317–318**, and **1795 Alsó Rhona pages 102–103, entry 4**. Inspect household
   remarks and any named relationships; the 1795 reference remains uncertain as No. 1 or 13. Numerical household
   counts cannot establish a parent or sibling by themselves.
4. **A complete readable Bréb cemetery survey or older photographs**, prioritizing names Yosef/Jozsef, Baruch/Burek,
   Katz/Kahan/Moldovan and the period suggested by the original household chronology. A father’s name must be read
   from a stone actually identified with the target, then corroborated where possible. A mother’s name requires its
   own evidence and cannot be supplied from a hypothetical sibling’s parentage.

The [Máramaros indexing project](https://www.jewishgen.org/databases/Hungary/Maramaros.htm) confirms that its registers
were photographed at Baia Mare. That is a concrete acquisition route for Book 1271 even though its original images
were not located online in this pass. No archive requests, correspondence, purchases or tree edits were made.

## Jozsef focused follow-up — 13 October 2026

**Neither parent is established.** Abraham’s family is set aside until an independent record connects it to Jozsef.
Book 1271’s original pages and the original 1838 widow entry remain unobtained. The new results are a precise acquisition
list, an older cemetery photograph, and clearer limits on the 1838 citation. **Yekutiel remains unattached.**

### Register 1271 acquisition and exact entries

The [Máramaros project inventory](https://www.jewishgen.org/databases/Hungary/Maramaros.htm), Book 1271 row, lists
Bréb births and deaths, 1823–1883, with 77 indexed records added in October 2014. Its acknowledgements identify
Dan Jurca as the photographer at Baia Mare and Sandy Malek as co-administrator; the published project contact is
**sandymalek@gmail.com**. This is a verified published contact, not confirmation that the mailbox is still monitored.
The Book 1271 row provides no image link. Google Drive links elsewhere in the inventory concern other books and
cannot be substituted for this register.

The [Sub-Carpathia image-request route](https://www.jewishgen.org/databases/Hungary/SubCarpathia.htm) is described
for that project's Ukrainian-held collection. It has not been established as an acquisition route for Romanian-held
Book 1271. Start with the Máramaros project and ask it to confirm the current archival shelfmark and image filenames.

These **index transcriptions**, recovered from the [WikiTree profile's source citations](https://www.wikitree.com/wiki/Moldovan_Katz-1),
identify the requested pages. They were read through the [public biography API](https://api.wikitree.com/api.php?action=getBio&key=Moldovan_Katz-1)
on 13 October 2026. They are not new readings of the original handwriting.

| Section and page-row | Event as cited | Father's name as indexed | Mother or identifying detail |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Births 02-05 | Sztrul, 10 June 1824 | MOLDOVAN, Joszif | Tsarna |
| Births 02-09 | Benyiamin, 19 February 1826 | MOLDIVAN, Burek / Jozip | Tsarna |
| Deaths 01-05 | Tsarna MOLDOVAN, 21 February 1826, age 38 | — | Spouse of Burek Josip |
| Births 02-11 | Hajim, 24 June 1827 | MOLDOVAN, Burek / Joszip | Rifke |
| Births 03-04 | Elle / Ilie, 7 November 1830 | MOLDOVAN, Joszip | Rifke |
| Births 03-10 | Volf / Lupu, 23 May 1833 | MOLDOVAN, Joszip | Rifke |
| Births 03-19 | Lazar, 10 May 1835 | MOLDOVAN, Joszip | Ilona / Rifke |
| Births 03-23 | Mingye, 26 February 1838 | MOLDOVAN, Josip | Ilona |

The slash readings require comparison with headings, neighboring names and marginal notes. They do not establish
that Burek is a patronymic, a second given name, or an alternative reading. Mingye's entry names a father but, without
the original wording, does not prove that he was alive on her birth or registration date. Prioritize deaths around
1835–1838, while requesting the whole death section to resolve the indexed 1826–1863 gap.

### What is and is not verified about the 1838 widow

The same profile reproduces a JewishGen citation: year **1838**, surname **KATZ**, given name **Joszip**, comment
**“widow of Joszip KATZ”**, town **Breb**, district **Kaszo**, county **Maramaros**. Its “record #14” follows the query
“Town (phonetically like): BREB,” retrieved 27 July 2024. It must not be used as an archival folio or household number.
No original page, household number or full shelfmark appears in that citation.

The [collection inventory](https://www.jewishgen.org/databases/Hungary/CensusOther.htm), Máramaros section, confirms
an 1838 Tolerance Tax dataset of **912 entries**. That verifies collection coverage, not this entry's handwriting or
identity. [Vivian Kahn and Rony Golan's source guide](https://avotaynuonline.com/2010/10/genealogical-sources-for-researching-jewish-families-in-maramaros-sziget-and-vicinity-by-vivian-kahn-and-rony-golan/),
“Census Records,” describes additional 1772–1843 and 1834–1843 tax material obtained from the Hungarian National
Archives. This is an acquisition lead, not an established shelfmark for the widow. Do not borrow the 1822 C 55 item
number for the 1838 list.

**Decision remains conditional:** an authentic 1838 entry identifying this Jozsef's widow would exclude the Seini
1840 stone as his grave. Until its date, wording and household are checked, the conflict remains unresolved.
Even a correction to the tax entry would not positively connect the Seini man to Jozsef.

### Older cemetery documentation found

The U.S. Commission for the Preservation of America's Heritage Abroad's
[Romania report photo appendix, page 6](https://www.heritageabroad.gov/wp-content/uploads/2022/10/Romania_Report_Pictures_2010.pdf#page=6)
contains a photograph captioned “Breb cemetery,” below the Borsa and Botiza photographs. The downloaded PDF page was
visually inspected: it shows leaning stones among vegetation, but the inscription is too indistinct to establish a
name, father or date. The file is identified as 2010; the date the photograph was taken is not established.
This is an older published image, not a full inscription survey.

The [site survey RO/MM/44](https://cdp.jewishgen.org/eastern-europe/romania/breb-maramure-judet), completed
30 June 2000 by John DeMetrick and Christina Crowder, records 40 visible stones and substantial obstruction by
vegetation. It explicitly says the surveyors have no further information and directs inquiries to the Jewish
community in Baia Mare or the Federation of Romanian Jewish Communities. Request the underlying RO/MM/44 photo
file and any inscription sheets through the cemetery project; do not assume that such sheets exist.

Charles Burns's [archived Bréb gallery](https://web.archive.org/web/20190714054024/http://galiciantraces.com/breb-romania/)
contains the four image references already recorded above. Its previously saved HTML footer supplies the historical
public contact **vallejo45@comcast.net**. Current deliverability is unverified. The useful request is for the complete
2018 Bréb set, full-resolution IMG_2576, IMG_2581, IMG_2582 and IMG_2584, and any transcription or location notes.

A separate photographer, Daniel Grünfeld, documents a visit on **13 May 2019**, image **4153**, in
[Parallel Traces](https://danielgruenfeld.de/parallel-traces/). His published work is an artistic multiple exposure,
not a named burial inventory. His [contact page](https://danielgruenfeld.de/info/) offers another route to ask whether
readable close-ups survive. No published material examined here identifies Jozsef's stone.

### One consolidated image request — prepared, not sent

**Proposed recipient:** Sandy Malek, Máramaros Jewish Records Indexing Project, at the published address above.

**Subject:** Bréb Register 1271 originals and source identification for the 1838 Joszip Katz widow entry

Dear Sandy,

I am researching Burek Jozsef/Joszip Moldovan Katz of Bréb (Breb/Brébb), Máramaros. His estimated death around 1837
and his parents remain unverified. Your project lists Book 1271, photographed at the Romanian National Archives,
Baia Mare branch, as Bréb births/deaths 1823–1883.

Could you provide access to the original images for the eight entries in the table above: births **02-05, 02-09,
02-11, 03-04, 03-10, 03-19, 03-23**, and Tsarna's death **01-05**? Please include full spreads, headings, margins,
annotations and neighboring rows. The key question is the actual handwriting behind “Burek / Jozip” and
“Burek / Joszip,” including any patronymic or alternative name omitted from the index.

Please also provide the surviving death section, especially pages covering **1835–1838**, plus the pages connecting
the indexed 1826 and 1863 entries. Is that gap in the original book, the photographs, or only the transcription?
Please include the cover and current archival shelfmark, original pagination and image identifiers.

A separate JewishGen Other Hungarian Census citation names **the widow of Joszip KATZ, Bréb, Kaszo district,
Máramaros, 1838**. The profile gives search-result #14 from a town-phonetic BREB query retrieved 27 July 2024, but no
archival page. Could you identify its source file, full archive reference, page and household, or direct me to the
census project holding the original? I need the dated heading and neighboring households as well as the entry.

If you know of a complete Bréb cemetery inscription survey or older photographs, please point me to its custodian;
existing leads are survey **RO/MM/44**, the Commission's Romania photo appendix **page 6**, and Charles Burns's
2018 Bréb photographs **IMG_2576/2581/2582/2584**. No stone has yet been identified with this man.

Please advise access conditions and any charges before arranging paid work. This request does not authorize fees.

### Coverage and remaining access limits for this pass

- The project inventory, collection description, public profile citations, published cemetery survey and photographic
  sources above were read. The 2010 photo appendix was downloaded and page 6 visually inspected.
- Original Book 1271 pages and the 1838 tax image were not obtained. Browser tools failed before reaching the existing
  authenticated research session; an authenticated JewishGen search did **not run** in this pass.
- A new Romanian archive search by **1271** reached only the HTML shell; the site's observed search request returned
  HTTP 403. No result set was inspected. This does not show that the book is absent from the catalog.
- A search-engine excerpt points to the Maramureș archival guide (2015), printed page 127, listing Bréb Israelite
  births/deaths 1823–1883. The [full PDF](https://www.biblioteca-digitala.ro/reviste/carte/Arhivele-Nationale-Serviciul-Judetean-Maramures/ghidul-fondurilor-colectiilor-arhivistice-transilvania-anterioare-1918-1919-maramures_11_2015.pdf)
  returned HTTP 403. Treat this as a catalog lead pending inspection, not evidence of continuous surviving entries.
- No correspondence, paid request, donation or family-tree edit was made. Parentage requires an identifiable original
  naming the relationship or a corroborated argument resolving competing identities; the mother needs separate evidence.

## Follow-up: the 1838 widow entry located in the live index — 13 October 2026

**Result:** The authenticated JewishGen search now confirms that the widow statement is present in the database
itself, with a precise page and entry. This supersedes the earlier browser-access limitation above. The original
handwritten page has **not** been obtained. The index alone neither proves the widow's husband is our Jozsef nor
identifies either of his parents.

### Exact indexed record

Source: [JewishGen Hungary Database](https://www.jewishgen.org/databases/Hungary/), collection **Other Hungarian
Census Records, 1770–1875**. Authenticated query: Town, phonetically like **BREB**, run 13 October 2026; 45 matching
records. The result table labels the locator **Page No./Record No.**

| Field | Indexed value |
|---|---|
| Town / district / county / year | Breb / Kaszo / Maramaros / 1838 |
| Name | KATZ, Joszip |
| Relationship | Blank |
| Page No./Record No. | **192/114** |
| Comments | **widow of Joszip KATZ** |
| Reference | Hungarian National Archives;Repartitio (Tolerance Tax); Kaszo, Maramaros 1838 |
| Database row identifier | `ccd996b4-17ca-41a8-9ad3-4fffd84fefcf_0017231` |

There is no image link in this result row and no complete archival shelfmark. The index gives no widow's first
name, ages, or named children here. Its record number is **114**, not a house number. The profile's older search
result ordinal **14** should not be used as an archival locator. The [collection documentation](https://www.jewishgen.org/databases/Hungary/CensusOther.htm)
explains that page numbers were apparently added later, and that record numbers are original entry numbers rather
than house numbers; the original image must establish how these apply to this sheet.

The same query returns sixteen Bréb entries for 1838, all indexed on page 192, entries **110–125**. The immediate
neighbors are **VIDDER, Jojm?**, 192/113, and **JUKOSZ?, Jakob**, 192/115. These are transcription spellings, useful
for locating the page rather than proposed family connections.

A second query, surname exactly **KATZ** AND given name exactly **Joszip**, returns four records: this widow entry
and Remete entries for 1827 (94/[7]), 1838 (121/17), and 1843 (211/15). These demonstrate the need to distinguish
namesakes; the Remete entries have not been attached to the Bréb family or to the Seini gravestone.

### What would make this decisive?

The original needs to confirm the name, widow designation, Bréb heading, and the date to which the list refers.
Then the household needs to be connected to Burek Jozsef/Joszip Moldovan Katz, ideally through the widow's name,
children, or continuity with another identifiable record. If both conditions hold, death before the 1838 entry
would exclude the Seini stone dated 1840 as his own grave. It would **not** establish an exact 1837 death or prove
his father's name. Yekutiel remains unattached, and the mother's identity remains unsupported.

### Original-image routes checked

- The target JewishGen row supplies the index locator above but no original image or complete archive reference.
- The [Hungarian National Archives finding-aids page](https://mnl.gov.hu/angol/mnl/ol/databases_finding_aids?theme=mnlvakbarat)
  points to the electronic archive portals. Attempts to open `eleveltar.hu` and `lnyr.eleveltar.hu` failed with
  connection errors. Their catalogs were not searched successfully; this is an access failure, not a negative
  holding result.
- The working [MNL AdatbázisokOnline](https://adatbazisokonline.mnl.gov.hu/) search for **Judaeorum** returned four
  E 156 Urbaria et Conscriptiones items dating to 1773, 1774, and 1784. None was the target 1838 list. This limited
  query does not establish whether another catalog route contains it.
- The Hungarian Jewish Museum & Archives series [HU HJMA XXIV-D-1, Összeírások fényképei](https://archives.milev.hu/index.php/osszeirasok-fenykepei;isad)
  describes photographic copies of National Archives censuses from 1813–1848. Its county/year inventory does not
  list Máramaros. The listed **Marosszék** is a different jurisdiction. No target image was found in this described
  series; the institution's other holdings have not been ruled out.

### Replacement paragraph for the unsent request above

Replace the earlier paragraph asking for an unidentified 1838 source with this precise request; retain the Book
1271 entry list and cemetery questions in the same draft:

> I have now located the JewishGen Other Hungarian Census index entry for **KATZ, Joszip; Breb, Kaszo district,
> Maramaros, 1838; page 192, record 114**, with the comment **“widow of Joszip KATZ.”** Its reference reads
> **“Hungarian National Archives;Repartitio (Tolerance Tax); Kaszo, Maramaros 1838”** and its database identifier is
> **ccd996b4-17ca-41a8-9ad3-4fffd84fefcf_0017231**. Please supply or identify the original full-page image, including
> the dated title/heading and column headings, page 192 with Bréb entries 110–125, and any continuation or reverse
> side. Adjacent entries are VIDDER, Jojm? (113) and JUKOSZ?, Jakob (115). Please include the complete current
> archival shelfmark and image identifier, and confirm whether 1838 is the assessment year or another date on the
> document. I need to read the widow designation and any personal names, household details, alternative name, or
> annotation omitted from the index. If another project holds the source photographs, please identify the
> custodian. Please advise access conditions and charges before arranging paid work; no fees are authorized.

**Status:** Draft prepared, not sent. No fees incurred. The advance is a directly checked index entry and a precise
image target, not original-record verification or proven parentage.

## Shalom Kahana descendant branch — follow-up begun 13 October 2026

At Hillel Kahana’s request, the existing [family tree](https://avihil1.github.io/kahan-family-tree/) was expanded
around his father Shalom Kahana (1943–1996). Eight named descendants were added: Hillel on his direct family
testimony, and Michal’s seven children on her [2019 interview](https://www.inn.co.il/news/406366).
Ruth and Yakir were linked as spouses. The [family memorial](https://sites.google.com/view/kahanas) supports
additional biographical details. Full citations, source limits and conflicts are in the
[tree’s research notes](https://github.com/avihil1/kahan-family-tree/blob/main/research/shalom-kahana.md).

This is not a complete descendant roster. The interview’s 13-child family conflicts with the previous Geni-derived
18-child count; the exact unnamed-child placeholder was removed pending resolution. Remaining children and later
generations require named parent-child links from the family or an accessible tree export. No candidate ancestors
were attached, and Jozsef’s parentage remains unresolved.


### Family testimony and parentage clarification — 14 October 2026

Hillel supplied thirteen siblings (including himself) and confirmed Rachel (1972–1972) as an additional child:
**14 children total**. He confirmed Ruth née Pasternak as mother of all fourteen, and named her parents Eliezer
and Erna. The tree now includes all fourteen named children; later descendant branches remain incomplete.
The roster and its family-testimony attribution are in the [existing tree research notes](https://github.com/avihil1/kahan-family-tree/blob/main/research/shalom-kahana.md).

The Dov Ber Bernat → Chaim Arye Leb Moldovan Kahan link was reviewed after Hillel asked about certainty.
The patronymic cited from JOWBR **J_MARAMURE_0008659** is Dov son of Chaim Aryeh HaKohen, Giulesti,
Rosh Chodesh Shevat 5688. The original inscription was not inspected in this review. Bernat's role as informant
in **1902-229** and the Junger in-law references offer collateral support but do not, by themselves, explicitly
establish that relationship. The link is retained with an evidence qualification, not described as certain.
See the [evidence audit and exact references](https://github.com/avihil1/kahan-family-tree/blob/main/research/shalom-kahana.md#dov-ber-parentage-evidence-audit).



## Follow-up: authenticated source verification, 14 October 2026

**Conclusion:** the proposed father–son relationship between Dov/Bernat Kahan and Chaim Arye Leb
Moldovan Kahan is supported by converging indirect evidence, but this pass does not establish it with
high certainty. Two identifications remain to be proved: the surname-less Dov burial is this Bernat,
and its father Chaim Aryeh is this particular Chaim Arye Leb. Freide Klein's motherhood requires
separate evidence; it cannot be inferred from the patronymic.

### Evidence inspected and what it establishes

| Source and exact reference | Finding | Evidentiary limit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| [JOWBR J_MARAMURE_0008659](https://www.jewishgen.org/databases/cemetery/jowbr.php?rec=J_MARAMURE_0008659), live authenticated record | Hebrew-name field: “Dov ben Chaim Aryeh HaKohen”; Giulesti; R.Ch.Shevat 5688; age 56. | Surname is blank; page explicitly says “No Image Available.” Confirms the indexed patronymic, not the gravestone reading or the identities of both men. |
| [Yad Vashem 3983900](https://collections.yadvashem.org/en/names/3983900), original Page of Testimony, signed 2 July 2000 | Grandson Dov Kahana names Rivka Kahana, maiden name Liberman, widow of Dov, resident GIULESTI. | Corroborates Dov's household when compared with Bernat/Rifke Liberman's children's records. Does not name Dov's father or use Bernat. |
| JewishGen Máramaros deaths: Brana Kahan, 5 January 1898, age 23 months; Inventory 1371, Book 53, **1898-10** | Parents Bernat Kahan and Regina Liberman; informant Izrael Junger described as father's brother-in-law. | Gives a real kinship connection, but “brother-in-law” alone does not identify which spouse supplies it. |
| Same collection: Leib Kahan, 24 June 1897, age 72; Inventory 1371, Book 53, **1897-76** | Parents Jozsef Kahan and Rifke Berkovics; informant Sztrul Junger described as deceased's son-in-law. | To connect the two death records, Izrael and Sztrul must be identified as the same man. Several Junger households occur locally. |
| Births: Riwke Kahan, 9 September 1874, Book 1098, **09-06** | Father Chaim/Leb Kahan, mother Freide. | Identifying this infant as the later Regina Kahan married to Izrael Junger remains an inference from name, age and town. |
| Births: Mozes/Mojsze Junger, 17 December 1896, Inventory 1371, Book 51, **1896-217**; Cheim Leb Junger, 7 December 1898, Book 52, **1898-222**; Eugenia Laura Junger, 20 October 1902, Inventory 1373, Book 35, **1902-240** | Parents Izrael Junger and Regina Kahan; mother's town Gyulafalu/Gyulafalva/Maragyulafalu. Ages broadly fit Riwke born 1874. | Stronger household identification than a surname match, but none of these entries names Regina's parents. |
| Deaths: Majer Jakab Kahan, November 1881, retrospectively registered **1902-229**, Inventory 1373, Book 41 | Parents Leb Kahan and Freide Klein; Bernat Kahan reported it. | Index does **not** say Bernat was a brother. Book 41 comes from the project transcription; JewishGen's displayed book field is blank. Contemporary death index: Book 1106, **14-02**, 12 November 1881. |
| Marriages: Ruchel Kahan / Smil Fogel, 14 August 1901, Bardfalu, Inventory 1373, **1901-26** | Bride's parents Chaim Leb and Freida Klein; witness Bernad Kahan, 30, Maragyulafalu. | Witnessing is not a stated sibling relationship. Bernath also witnesses INDIG/SAJOVICS marriage **1902-43**, so witnessing alone is weak evidence. |

The civil-register references above were read in the authenticated JewishGen Hungary database.
[Collection and repository description](https://www.jewishgen.org/databases/Hungary/Maramaros.htm).
Books 53 and 41 are also identified by the saved project transcriptions:
[1895–1898 deaths](https://docs.google.com/spreadsheets/d/1tgtHd1kNIv1LFaOxtoKFuVqqmFE9ln8XJznPyRHGVAg/)
and [1902–1904 deaths](https://docs.google.com/spreadsheets/d/1-TKkd3ZBUfwgYO1MUkizdgUqmZyTIZ7W7dPbSKqwLWE/).
The spreadsheet and JewishGen versions of one entry are not independent sources.

### Original testimony and conflicts

[Original Rivka testimony image](https://assets.yadvashem.org/image/upload/t_f_image/f_auto/q_auto/v1/remote_media/arch1_yadvashem/200007301604_267_7590/43.jpg)
was visually inspected. Relevant handwriting: victim “כהנא”, “רבקה”, maiden name “ליברמן”,
husband “דב”, status “אלמנה”, relationship of submitter “נכד”. It supplies approximate birth 1880
and age 65 at death in 1944, which are themselves approximate/inconsistent and conflict with earlier
civil ages. It records ten children, but this count was not used to create unnamed descendants.
Its blank parental fields refer to Rivka, not Dov.

Bernat's recorded ages vary: 32 in 1896; 32 in 1900; 34 in 1902; 35 in 1903; 34 in 1905;
30 as a witness in 1901. These imply roughly 1863–1871, while the proposed burial's age 56
in 1928 implies roughly 1871–1872. Thus the tree's previous unqualified birth year 1868 is
an estimate derived from some records, not a located birth entry. WikiTree also has a 1927/1928
discrepancy between its narrative and death field.

[Abraham Wolf's profile Kahan-390](https://www.wikitree.com/wiki/Kahan-390) explicitly infers his sibling
relationship from his son Mojse's death, **1903-81**, Inventory 1373, Book 41: Bernad is called
the deceased's uncle. That supports a collateral relationship; Abraham's linked father cannot
then be reused as independent proof of Bernat's father. The linked
[Gyulafalu research narrative](https://www.wikitree.com/wiki/Space:Kahan_in_Gyulafalu_%28Giule%C8%99ti%29)
is a research hypothesis, not another original establishing this parentage.

### Search coverage and access status

Reviewed sources on [Kahan-376](https://www.wikitree.com/wiki/Kahan-376#Sources) and
[Moldovan_Kahan-1](https://www.wikitree.com/wiki/Moldovan_Kahan-1#Sources), collateral profiles,
and live JewishGen Máramaros births/deaths/marriages on 14 October 2026.

All pages were retrieved for these town queries (all dates):
- GYULAFALU phonetic: births 220, deaths 68, marriages 19.
- GYULAFALVA exact: births 65, deaths 2, marriages 1.
- MARAGYULAFALU exact: births 57, deaths 24, marriages 5.

These result sets overlap. The initial attempted surname constraint did not appear in the results
heading and was not effective; these are town-wide searches, not surname-filtered counts.
Relevant Kahan/Moldovan/Junger entries and Ber/Berl/Dov/Bernat variants were reviewed.
No direct birth, marriage or death entry naming Bernat's own parents was identified in this pass.
This does not establish that such a record is absent from the archive or all indexes.

The login succeeded. The earlier access-failure note is superseded for JOWBR: the record itself now
explicitly reports no image available there. Civil index rows examined supplied no original-image link.
The project describes archive photography and the Baia Mare repository; an original was not obtained
for the decisive civil entries. Do not equate that with an original being lost or undigitized.

### Precise request prepared, not sent

To the Máramaros Jewish Records Indexing Project / Romanian National Archives, Baia Mare branch:

> Please provide, or identify the access route to, full-page original images including headings,
> margins and continuation pages for the following Giulești/Gyulafalu and Bárdfalu entries:
