> (2) Brana Kahan death, 1898-10, Inventory 1371, Book 53;
> (3) Leib Kahan death, 1897-76, Inventory 1371, Book 53;
> (4) Riwke Kahan birth, 9 September 1874, Book 1098, page/entry 09-06;
> (5) Ruchel Kahan / Smil Fogel marriage, 1901-26, Inventory 1373, Bárdfalu.
> The question is whether Bernat/Bernad Kahan, partner of Rifke/Regina Liberman, is explicitly
> identified as a son of Chaim Leb Kahan, or as a brother of Majer Jakab/Riwke/Ruchel.
> Please preserve the exact relationship wording for Izrael/Sztrul Junger. If an original
> marriage entry for Izrael Junger and Regina/Riwke Kahan is indexed elsewhere, its parents'
> fields would also resolve the collateral connection. Please advise availability before any fees.

For JOWBR, request the underlying transcription or photograph for J_MARAMURE_0008659, including
any surname, full inscription, plot identifier and provenance omitted from the online record.
No correspondence was sent and no fees were incurred.



### Additional original testimony: Rifka KAHAN, reported by her son Menachem

[Open Menachem alongside his brother Chaim Arye Leb Kahana in the tree](https://avihil1.github.io/kahan-family-tree/tree.html#Kahan-398). His existing node now includes the Hebrew name מנחם כהן; the direct link opens the folded branch.

Added 14 October 2026 at Hillel's request. [Yad Vashem Page of Testimony 1625122](https://collections.yadvashem.org/en/names/1625122)
and its [original image](https://assets.yadvashem.org/image/upload/t_f_image/f_auto/q_auto/v1/remote_media/arch1_yadvashem/25021237_216_1711/95.jpg)
were inspected. In the victim's family-name field the handwriting reads **KAHAN** in Latin letters and
**כהן** in Hebrew; her given name is **Rifka / רבקה**. The submitter writes **כהן מנחם** and describes himself
as **בן** (son), explicitly naming **רבקה הנ״ל** as his mother. The database indexes him as Menakhem Kohen.
Hillel identifies Menachem as his grandfather's brother; this family identification is testimony from Hillel,
distinct from the document's explicit mother–son relationship.

This is direct original-document evidence that her own son used **Kahan / כהן** for his mother.
It corroborates the Kahan spelling in the civil records and, compared with grandson Dov's later testimony
[3983900](https://collections.yadvashem.org/en/names/3983900), which writes **כהנא**, documents surname variants
within this family. The spelling is in the original, not merely Yad Vashem's English transliteration.
It does not mean Kahana was never used, nor does a shared spelling prove a specific earlier ancestor.

Further identifying fields: maiden name **Lieberman / ליברמן**, birthplace **Sat-Sugatag**, residence **Giulești**,
father **שמעון מנחם** (Shimon Menachem), mother **צפורה** (Tzipora), age **72**, ten children, and death in Auschwitz
in 1944. These are details reported by her son, not separately verified vital records. Sat-Sugatag and Lieberman
corroborate the civil household of Bernat and Regina/Rifke Liberman of Falu-Sugatag.
Age 72 in 1944 implies birth about 1871–1872, consistent with the earlier civil ages more closely than the grandson's
approximate 1880. The husband-name field is blank; this sheet does not independently name Dov/Bernat or his father.
The proposed Dov → Chaim Arye Leb relationship therefore retains its existing qualification.




## Shimon Menachem Kahan research — 14 October 2026

**The official Netanya burial register resolves the death-date conflict: 2 January 1973, not 1974.**
The record names **כהן, שמעון (מנחם)**, father **דב**, and gives death and burial on **כ״ח טבת תשל״ג**,
with civil burial date **02/01/1973**. Grave: **block ה, section ד, row 10, place 1**.
[Official Chevra Kadisha Netanya search](https://ck-natanya.co.il/search-deceased/): free-name query
**שמעון מנחם כהן**, one result, all columns inspected. Hillel supplied a matching screenshot on 14 October 2026.
[Block ה cemetery map](https://ck-natanya.co.il/wp-content/uploads/pdfMaps/map%D7%94.pdf).
The Hebrew date conversion was independently checked with
[Hebcal](https://www.hebcal.com/converter?hy=5733&hm=Tevet&hd=28&h2g=1).

### Original family evidence

| Record | Original wording / finding | What it supports |
| --- | --- | --- |
| [Rifka 1625122](https://collections.yadvashem.org/en/names/1625122) | Submitter כהן מנחם calls himself her son; victim KAHAN / כהן, Rifka / רבקה, née Lieberman. | Explicit mother–son relationship and surname forms. |
| [Abraham Lieber 1501887](https://collections.yadvashem.org/en/names/1501887) | Submitter כהן מנחם calls himself the victim's brother. Father is **דב / Bernat**, mother **רבקה / Reghina**, birthplace Giulești, birth 1911. | Explicit brother relationship; importantly, directly pairs the Hebrew and civil names of the same parents. |
| [Frimed Frida Berkovits 1600529](https://collections.yadvashem.org/en/names/1600529) | Submitter כהן מנחם calls himself her husband. Born 1916 in Oknasugatag; father **מנחם / Mendel**, mother **אלטי חיה**. Child rows name **פרידה, 1940**, and **טובה בלומה, 1942**. | Direct testimony by husband/father for the wife and the two daughters already in the tree. |
| [USC testimony 15743](https://vha.usc.edu/testimony/15743?mm=peo) | Henyah Helen Fishkovits née Berkovits, born 16 August 1919, interviewed in Hebrew on 28 May 1996 in Rehovot; person index identifies Fridah Kahan as biological sister. | A separately supplied collateral-family account for the wife's family; metadata inspected, not a transcription of the spoken testimony. |

The [original Abraham Lieber sheet](https://assets.yadvashem.org/image/upload/t_f_image/f_auto/q_auto/v1/remote_media/arch1_yadvashem/25021218_207_1709/20.jpg)
was visually inspected: **דב** and **Bernat** occur in the same father's-name field, and **רבקה / Reghina**
in the mother's field. This is a stronger identity bridge between Dov and Bernat than merely treating the names
as plausible equivalents. It does not name Dov's father Chaim Aryeh or uniquely identify the surname-less JOWBR grave;
the earlier ancestral-parentage qualification remains.

The [original wife-and-children sheet](https://assets.yadvashem.org/image/upload/t_f_image/f_auto/q_auto/v1/remote_media/arch1_yadvashem/16021625_265_0482/180.jpg)
was inspected, including the child rows and the submitter's **בעלה** (her husband) statement.
It carries a handwritten submission date **12.1.56**; the later **10.5.57** mark at the top is not adopted as its
submission date. WikiTree's general statement that these testimonies were submitted in 1955 does not describe
this sheet's dated signature. The wife's family name is BERKOVITS in the original, while the submitter signs כהן מנחם.

[Yad Vashem 8619748](https://collections.yadvashem.org/en/names/8619748), indexed as Tova Bluma Berkovits,
uses the **same 1600529 image**, and is therefore not an independent source or another child.
The handwritten child rows, rather than the derived surname, support the existing Kahan daughters.

The wife's WikiTree page links a
[JewishGen USC interview record, USCINTERV_0015463](https://www.jewishgen.org/databases/glue_s2.php?test=Y&rec=USCINTERV_0015463),
which was read after login and followed to USC interview **15743**. The JewishGen row identifier and USC interview
number differ. Its family index names father Menachem Berkovits, mother Chayah-Ilonkah Berkovits, and sister Fridah
Kahan born 1916. USC's People index additionally lists nieces **Mirelah Kahan** and **Belimeleh Kahan**.
Belimeleh may correspond to Blima, but these niece identities were not assigned to the two girls without
the relevant spoken segments. Exact discriminating segments: Fridah **6 and 135**, Mirelah **12, 15, 22, 26, 135**,
Belimeleh **12**, parents **9, 35, 134**. The mother's Alti Chaya / Chayah-Ilonkah forms require comparison;
no additional ancestor nodes were created from this index.

### Remaining conflicts and access coverage

[WikiTree Kahan-398](https://www.wikitree.com/wiki/Kahan-398) gives birth about **1913** in the heading,
about **1900** in the biography, and cites a gravestone age **70**, which would imply about **1902–1903**
if this 1973 burial is the cited person. None is a verified birth entry. The tree retains about 1913
with an explicit uncertainty/conflict note; the official death date is corrected to **1973-01-02**.

The cited [Bezikaron grave URL](https://bezikaron.co.il/%D7%A9%D7%9E%D7%A2%D7%95%D7%9F%20%D7%9E%D7%A0%D7%97%D7%9D-%D7%9B%D7%94%D7%9F-na-na-j3Lzq/)
now returns HTTP 404. Internet Archive queries for the old slug did not recover a photograph (one www query
timed out); the original stone was not inspected. The official burial register independently corroborates the
Hebrew name, father's name and 1973 date, without corroborating the quoted age 70.
Live Geni profile access returned a blank page; the previously saved public search listing says 1913–1974 and
is not an independent vital record.

The original wife testimony and both daughters' WikiTree citations were read, as were the mother and brother
testimonies, USC metadata and the complete one-row official cemetery search result. No new birth or marriage
register search was performed for Menachem. No later marriage or additional descendants were established.
Next: obtain a readable photograph or burial file for block ה / section ד / row 10 / place 1, asking for age
and any birth date; locate the birth or marriage record distinguishing the competing birth estimates.
No request was sent and no fees were incurred.



## Chaim Arye Leb Kahana: original 1903 birth and search for his children — 14 October 2026

Hillel clarified that the requested children are those of his grandfather **Chaim Arye Leb Kahana (1903–1956)**,
not the earlier Leb born about 1827. No additional child has been established in this research pass.

### Original birth entry obtained and inspected

The [Romanian National Archives catalog](https://descopera.arhivelenationale.ro/cota/?cid=13914516)
identifies **Giuleşti, Registru născuţi, înregistrat la Berbeşti**, 1902–1903, current reference
**MM-F-00743-1-1-00236**, explicitly giving former reference **35**. This resolves the JewishGen Book 35 locator.
The catalog lists finding aid MM-I-01480; JewishGen uses the earlier Baia Mare Inventory 1373 reference.

The decisive original is **entry 64, folio 176v**, scan **MM-FD-00743-1-1-236_0352.TIFF**,
in **MM-FD-00743-1-1-236_03.PDF, PDF page 57**.
[Full archive PDF, page 57](https://descopera.arhivelenationale.ro/api/media/file?id=13914516&type=fisiere&mediaId=488883#page=57).
[Single original page for convenient viewing](https://avihil1.github.io/kahan-family-tree/research/leb-1903-birth.pdf).
[English translation of the complete original page](https://avihil1.github.io/kahan-family-tree/research/leb-1903-birth-en.html). Includes the declaration, parent and child fields, closing statement and certification; uncertain handwriting is marked. This translation derives from the same original and is not independent evidence.

The latter is a one-page extraction, with the complete page preserved; it is not another independent source.

| Field | Reading from the original |
|---|---|
| Child | Leb, male, Jewish religion |
| Birth | 10 April 1903, Máragyulafalu |
| Registration | 16 April 1903, Bárdfalva |
| Father | Káhán Bernad/Bernat; final letter uncertain; Jewish, smallholder, age 35 |
| Father's residence and birthplace | Máragyulafalu |
| Mother | Liberman Regina, Jewish, age 32 |
| Mother's residence / birthplace | Máragyulafalu / Falusugatag |
| Informant / remarks | Child's father; remarks field blank |

The registration date explains the **16 April** date in the volume's alphabetical index, where the name is
rendered approximately “Kalian Lab”; the birth date on the actual entry is **10 April**. The JewishGen index
entry **23e75e42-64cc-4287-b44a-0326952f5089_0038136**, Book 35, **1903-64**, was retrieved in the logged-in session.
Its parents and ages agree with the original. The index and original are one underlying event.

This is direct evidence for Leb's parents **Bernad/Bernat Kahan and Regina Liberman**. The separate, previously
inspected [Abraham Lieber testimony](https://collections.yadvashem.org/en/names/1501887) pairs the father's
Hebrew/civil names Dov/Bernat and the mother's Rivka/Reghina. This supports the household identification
already in the tree. Ages imply approximate birth years only. No grandparents are named; this page does not
resolve Bernat's proposed father or Jozsef's parents.

### Children: coverage checked, births still unresolved

The existing children are **Penina Perl (1929), Dov/Bernard (1930), and Shalom (1943)**. Their archival birth
entries have not been obtained. The 1951 immigration lists already cited in the tree support Dov's
Chaim/Leah parent names and Shalom's household with Leib/Leah; they are immigration evidence, not birth registers.
Shalom's Timișoara birthplace is from the family memorial. Dov's biography gives Transylvania generally;
Penina's exact birthplace is not established here. Do not assign all three a Timișoara birthplace.

The [Máramaros project inventory](https://www.jewishgen.org/databases/hungary/Maramaros.htm) lists the
Berbești-area birth volumes through **1907–1913**, not the children's 1929–1943 period.
The [Romania vital-record inventory](https://docs.google.com/spreadsheets/d/1UW157jQ79bfyXGDstS4oMcePXXKZzx2ddtwxwbylzjY/edit)
lists 31 towns and no Timișoara birth-register series. This is a coverage limitation of that inventory,
not evidence that the children's registrations do not exist.

Authenticated JewishGen searches used **Town, phonetically like TIMISOARA**, all dates:

- Romania Vital Records Births: **50 results, all 50 examined**.
- Hungarian Births: **14 results, all 14 examined**.
- Máramaros Vital Records Births: **56 results, both pages examined**.
- Romanian Censuses of Jewish Residents, 1939–1945: **138 results, all three pages examined**.

No returned entry identifies a child of this Chaim Arye Leb and Leah. The town search also returns later
Timișoara marriage/death annotations on births elsewhere; its result count is not a count of Timișoara births.
It does not rule out records indexed under another town, surname, or in an unindexed volume.

### Cemetery leads remain unattached

The **JOWBR Romania–Banat** search **Surname, phonetically like KAHAN**, all dates, returned **391 records**;
all eight pages were examined. Among the Timișoara surname matches:

| Indexed person | Death | Exact reference | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| Ferenc Kahan | 4 April 1943 | [J_BANAT_0013433](https://www.jewishgen.org/databases/cemetery/jowbr.php?rec=J_BANAT_0013433), CET register VOL_1, p. 122 | Age and parents blank; no image |
| Rozalia Kahan | 27 August 1941 | [J_BANAT_0013434](https://www.jewishgen.org/databases/cemetery/jowbr.php?rec=J_BANAT_0013434), CET register VOL_1, p. 122 | Age and parents blank; no image |

Other Kahan matches include Benedict (3 September 1941), Roza Freund née Kahan (29 August 1937),
Hermann (1922), Hermann's wife née Kahn (23 July 1939), Rozalia (14 June 1957), and David (2003, age 90).
The two p. 122 entries are specific acquisition leads, **not identified children**. Their placement on one page
does not prove kinship. Family memory of two deceased children is not sufficient to attach these names.

[Cemetery ROM-06473 metadata](https://www.jewishgen.org/databases/cemetery/jowbrshow.php?id=ROM-06473)
locates the registers at the **Jewish Community of Timișoara**, Calea Sever Bocu cemetery. Volume 1 spans
1865–1959. The individual entries explicitly say no image available; the originals remain uninspected.

### Next original requests (prepared, not sent)

For the Jewish Community of Timișoara: “Please supply the complete original page **CET register VOL_1,
page 122**, including headings and any continuation or annotations, containing **Ferenc Kahan,
4 April 1943**, and **Rozalia Kahan, 27 August 1941**. We need ages, parents, address and grave/entry references
to determine whether either belongs to the household of **Chaim Arye/Leib Kahan (born 10 April 1903,
Máragyulafalu) and wife Leah**. Please also identify the source entry for **Benedict Kahan, 3 September 1941,
JOWBR J_BANAT_0002513**. No relationship is assumed.”

The Romanian archive catalog and **TM-ID-01194.pdf, PDF pages 112–113** identify specific Jewish registers:
[Finding aid PDF](https://descopera.arhivelenationale.ro/api/media/file?id=7372851&type=fisiere&mediaId=581455#page=112).

- **Cetate births, 1862–1942:** TM-F-00004-01194-417-4
  ([catalog](https://descopera.arhivelenationale.ro/cota/?cid=7614097)); alphabetical index **417-9**, 1886–1942
  ([catalog](https://descopera.arhivelenationale.ro/cota/?cid=7614103)).
- **Josefin births, 1887–1942:** finding-aid reference **TM-F-00004-419-4**; alphabetical index **419-8**.
- **Plasa Centrală births, 1886–1950:** finding-aid reference **TM-F-00004-416-4**; birth index **416-7**.

The finding aid and catalog describe registers, not entries naming this family. Cetate and Josefin cover the
reported 1929/1930 birth years if the children were registered there; they end before Shalom's 1943 birth.
The Centrală volume is a further jurisdictional lead, not proof that it covers his birthplace.

Prepared archive request: “Please check the alphabetical birth indexes **417-9 (Cetate), 419-8 (Josefin),
and 416-7 (Plasa Centrală)**, finding aid **1194**, for **Kahan/Kahana/Cohen**, father
**Chaim Arye/Leib/Leb (born 10 April 1903, Máragyulafalu)** and mother **Leah**. Known children:
**Penina/Perl, reported born 1929; Dov/Bernard, reported born 1930; Shalom, reported born 1943 in Timișoara**.
For matching entries, supply complete original pages from **417-4, 419-4 or 416-4**, including headings and
annotations. Please identify the municipal/communal series covering the 1943 Timișoara birth if it is outside
these registers. No birthplace is assumed for Penina or Dov, and no names are assumed for other children.”
No correspondence was sent, no fees incurred, and no candidate child added to the tree.

The Cetate birth-volume and alphabetical-index catalog pages were loaded and inspected on 14 October 2026;
neither exposed a MEDIA/files attachment. Their original pages have therefore not been searched.
This does not establish whether scans can be supplied by the archive.


## Children’s registration evidence: originals checked again — 14 October 2026

**There is registration evidence for Shalom and Dov. Their Romanian birth entries are still unlocated.**
The distinction is between an identifiable immigration registration naming the person and a contemporary birth record.
The original arrival pages were downloaded and visually inspected in this follow-up, rather than relying only
on the earlier tree citations.

| Person | Original registration | What it establishes |
|---|---|---|
| Shalom | Israel State Archives **GL-15496/1**, scan/PDF page **167**, printed list page **9**, **1 February 1951**, ship **Transylvania**, rows **480–482** | Row 480: **כהן לייב**, born **1903**, parents **דב/רבקה**; row 481: **לאה**, born **1907**; row 482: **שלום**, born **1943**. The surname, arrival number and destination are entered on the household’s first row. Shalom is grouped with Leib and Leah, supporting the household relationship; his row has no separately filled parent-name cell. |
| Dov/Bernard | Israel State Archives **GL-15497/1**, scan/PDF page **175**, printed list page **9**, **6 August 1951**, ship **Transylvania**, row **474** | **כהנא ברנרד**, born **1930**, parent-name column **חיים/לאה**, immigrant number **49306**, destination **ת.א.** (Tel Aviv). This explicitly records Chaim and Leah as Bernard’s parents. Identification with Dov uses the existing family/biographical context; the entry itself says Bernard. |

[Shalom’s complete original arrival page](https://journaliststudio.google.com/backlight/serve/image?document_id=ec7469b5b0ffa001_585158a3cc8e02af&page_number=166&width=800).
[Document viewer](https://journaliststudio.google.com/pinpoint/document-view?collection=585158a3cc8e02af&p=1&docid=ec7469b5b0ffa001_585158a3cc8e02af&dapvm=2).
[Dov/Bernard’s complete original arrival page](https://journaliststudio.google.com/backlight/serve/image?document_id=c7cab0afcb40306b_585158a3cc8e02af&page_number=174&width=800).
[Document viewer](https://journaliststudio.google.com/pinpoint/document-view?collection=585158a3cc8e02af&p=1&docid=c7cab0afcb40306b_585158a3cc8e02af&dapvm=2).
The image service numbers pages from zero: 166 and 174 correspond to viewer pages 167 and 175.
The viewer’s `p` query did not select page 167 when tested; use the direct image links for these pages.

These originals corroborate the existing children; they do not supply a complete child roster, Penina’s birth
entry, or the names of the children remembered as having died in Timișoara. No new child was inferred.

### A better documented Romanian birth-register route

The [Leo Baeck Institute / Yerusha survey, item 2421](https://jbat.lbi.org/items/show/2421), describes
**Plasa centrală Timișoara, birth book 4, 1886–1950**, as containing births in the town as well as the district,
including parents, their birthplaces, and often the child’s Hebrew name. This makes it relevant to the 1943
search, without proving that Shalom is included. Its access note reflects the survey in **2016**, not a newly
verified access decision today.

The associated [alphabetical index, item 2424](https://jbat.lbi.org/items/show/2424), is **book 7**, 39 sheets.
Its locator refers to the original register page number, which can differ from the archive’s later pagination.
The index title and description give 1886–1950; its separate Date field says “1886–1850”, an apparent catalog typo.
The previously cited official finding aid also gives 1886–1950. Neither LBI page supplies the register images.

Highest-value next check: **Timiș archives, fonds 4, inventory 1194, Plasa centrală birth index 7
(TM-F-00004-416-7)**, letter K and surname variants Kahan/Kahana/Cohen. Look for Penina/Perl (reported 1929),
Dov/Bernard (1930), and Shalom (1943), then obtain the matching original entries from **birth book 4
(TM-F-00004-416-4)** and compare parents Leib/Chaim Arye and Leah. This refines the already prepared archive
request; no correspondence was sent or fee incurred.

### Additional authenticated JewishGen searches completed

All used **Surname, phonetically like KAHAN**, all dates, from the **Romania database** results page.
Parent-name, given-name, date and place clues were compared within the returned records:

- **Hungarian Births:** 486 results, all 10 pages examined.
- **Romanian Emigration Lists, 1947–1948:** 87 results, both pages examined.
- **Registries of Romanian Survivors Returning from the Shoah:** 53 results, both pages examined.
- **New Romanian Lists:** 539 results, all 11 pages examined.

No returned record identifies an additional child of this Leib/Chaim Arye and Leah. These searches expand
the earlier town-only pass; they do not exhaust every surname spelling, every country collection, or
unindexed originals. The Sighet survivor named **Chaim Kahan**, born **15 May 1926**, is an electrician with
mother **Berta Ingber** and is not the grandfather born 10 April 1903; it was not merged into this household.

Official catalog locators: [Plasa Centrală birth index 7](https://descopera.arhivelenationale.ro/cota/?cid=7614092), **TM-F-00004-01194-416-7**; [birth book 4](https://descopera.arhivelenationale.ro/cota/?cid=7614088), **TM-F-00004-01194-416-4**. These retain inventory 1194 in the current reference.

Both official catalog pages loaded successfully but expose no MEDIA/files attachment; the birth book and index originals were not inspected.


## Hungarian archive route checked — 14 October 2026

Hungarian catalogs are a useful supplementary route for microfilms, duplicate registers, and records of
family members who lived in Hungary. The language of the 1903 record does not by itself establish present
custody in Hungary. The identified originals for Giuleşti/Berbeşti and Timișoara are in Romanian archives.

The [Hungarian National Archives microfilm-register guide](https://adatbazisokonline.mnl.gov.hu/adatbazis/mikrofilm-anyakonyvek/informacio)
says its collection is mainly religious registers through 1895, with some later volumes and duplicate copies.
It states that the digital microfilm images are viewed in MNL reading rooms; the public catalog is searchable.

The actual [MNL microfilm catalog](https://adatbazisokonline.mnl.gov.hu/adatbazis/mikrofilm-anyakonyvek/kereses)
was searched by town keyword. **Temesvár** and **Timișoara** returned explicit no-result pages.
Queries **Temesvar, Máragyulafalu, Máragyulafalva, Bárdfalva, Gyulafalva, Bréb** instead encountered
maintenance notices or HTTP 503 responses. Those checks did not complete and are not negative findings.
No relevant Hungarian-held register or new family entry was established in this pass.

The search-engine match [Bácsgyulafalva, MNL microfilm record 24558](https://adatbazisokonline.mnl.gov.hu/adatbazis/mikrofilm-anyakonyvek/adatlap/24558)
is Roman Catholic, for modern Telečka in Serbia, and is not Máragyulafalu/Giuleşti in Máramaros.
It must not be substituted for the family's birthplace.

[FamilySearch's Hungary Jewish Vital Records Index](https://www.familysearch.org/en/search/collection/1787825)
explicitly says its index is also on JewishGen. The collection title's 1800–1945 range does not establish
coverage of this family's towns and years, and a duplicated index would not be independent confirmation.
Its collection description was read; no new person search or original-image search there was completed.

For the children's actual birth entries, the highest-value identified source remains **Timișoara Plasa
Centrală, inventory 1194, birth book 4 and alphabetic index 7**, covering 1886–1950, in the Romanian Timiș archive.
Retry the failed Hungarian town queries when the catalog works. Prepared MNL question, not sent:
“Do your holdings include Jewish or civil-register originals, duplicates, or microfilms for **Máragyulafalu /
Máragyulafalva (Giuleşti), Bárdfalva (Berbeşti), Máramaros**, or **Temesvár (Timișoara)**? We seek the family of
**Káhán/Kahan Bernad/Bernat and Liberman Regina**, their son **Leb, born 10 April 1903**, and his children with
**Leah**, reported born 1929, 1930 and 1943. Please identify the precise register and date coverage.”
No correspondence was sent and no fees incurred.


## Penina Miller: original arrival record — 14 October 2026

**Penina (Perl) Miller, née Kahana, arrived on 17 November 1960 aboard the T. Herzl
(Theodor Herzl), with her husband and two daughters.** This is an inspected original immigrant list,
not a date inferred from her husband's biography.

**Exact reference:** Israel State Archives, immigrant lists October–December 1960, **GL-15501/6**,
file **000a6bq**, Pinpoint document **c2550aca54dbf1e2_585158a3cc8e02af**, **scan 84 of 175,
printed page 2, rows 89–92**. The preceding **scan 83, printed page 1**, supplies the full heading:
`רשימת העולים שהגיעו באניה ת.הרצל ביום 17.11.60`.
Scan 84 repeats `ת.הרצל 17.11.60` at its head.

- Row 89: **מילר — פרידריך (פנחס)**, born **1919**, parents **אשר / אסתר**, Romania,
  immigrant-certificate number **150204**.
- Row 90: **פנינה**, born **1929**, immediately beneath him in the same household.
- Rows 91–92: two daughters in that household. They are not added as public named tree nodes in this update.
- The household destination is **מלון אקדמאים, נצרת** (Academics' Hotel, Nazareth).

**Identity assessment: high confidence.** Penina's given name and 1929 birth year, her husband's surname,
Pinchas/Friedrich given names, 1919 birth year and father Asher, Romanian origin and Nazareth destination
agree with the existing Penina–Rabbi Pinchas Miller family. Her own surname and parent names are not
repeated in row 90; the household grouping identifies her as his wife. The husband's
[biography](https://he.wikipedia.org/wiki/פנחס_מילר) independently identifies his wife as Penina Kahana,
daughter of Chaim Arye Kahana, and places his aliyah in Hebrew year 5721, which includes November 1960.
The original supplies the exact day. This does not establish Penina's exact birth date or birthplace,
or a complete list of children throughout their marriage.

[Archival viewer](https://journaliststudio.google.com/pinpoint/document-view?collection=585158a3cc8e02af&docid=c2550aca54dbf1e2_585158a3cc8e02af&dapvm=2) · [Original scan 84](https://journaliststudio.google.com/backlight/serve/image?document_id=c2550aca54dbf1e2_585158a3cc8e02af&page_number=83&width=800) ·
[Preserved scan 84](penina-1960-arrival-84.png) · [Heading on scan 83](penina-1960-arrival-83.png).

**Search coverage:** searched the State Archives Pinpoint collection by quoted surname and given name,
then by surname and 1919 and the 1960 label. The exact phrase `מילר פנחס` gave no indexed matches;
the broader query found `פרידריך (פנחס)` in the October–December 1960 volume. Both original scans 83–84
were inspected, including headings and neighboring household rows. The supplied Geni profile loaded
an empty browser page, so no new profile details were extracted from it. No correspondence or fees.


## Penina and Dov: Sighet birthplace supplied by family — 14 October 2026

**Hillel Kahana states that Penina/Perl Kahana (Miller), born 1929, was born in Sighetu Marmației,
Maramureș, Romania, as was her brother Dov/Bernard Kahana, born 1930.** This is direct family testimony,
not a newly located birth certificate. It supersedes the earlier “birthplace not established” search assumption
for both siblings. Their exact birth dates remain unverified. Shalom's Timișoara birthplace is unchanged.
The existing tree entries now give Sighet with this source qualification.

**The birth-record search should therefore start in Sighet (historical Máramarossziget), not Timișoara.**
The Timișoara books previously identified remain relevant to Shalom and the family's later residence;
they are no longer the first choice for Penina's 1929 birth.

### What was checked

The [official Sighet town descriptor](https://descopera.arhivelenationale.ro/descriptor/?did=9685482)
loaded in the browser. Set its page size to 100 and examined **all 42 linked catalog descriptions**.
The birth-volume date ranges shown there run from 1895 to **1920**, not 1929 or 1930.
This is a defined catalog check, not proof that later registers do not survive or are absent from other series.

Opened the latest listed birth volume, [MM-F-00743-1-1-00650](https://descopera.arhivelenationale.ro/cota/?cid=13914930):
**29 December 1918–30 December 1920**, former number **19**, inventory **MM-I-01480**, under
**Exemplarul 2** (second copy). Its page exposes an index PDF and seven scan PDFs, but this book's stated
dates do not cover either sibling, so it was not searched for their births. The separate summary text says
1864–1915, conflicting with its title/date fields; that catalog inconsistency was not silently resolved.
The page labels this register category as protected for 100 years; this is not a verified access ruling
on a 1929 record. No 1929 or 1930 book reference was identified in this pass.

The [JewishGen Máramaros project inventory](https://www.jewishgen.org/databases/Hungary/Maramaros.htm)
was read for coverage. Its published table, labeled June 2018, does not list a 1929 birth volume;
this cannot rule out later additions or unindexed records. An attempted direct download of inventory 1469
(first-copy civil registers) returned HTML rather than the PDF; that finding aid was **not inspected**.

### Precise request prepared; not sent

To the Maramureș branch of the Romanian National Archives, with referral to **Sighetu Marmației's
Stare Civilă** if those years remain with the municipality:

“Please identify the current custodian and reference of the Sighetu Marmației civil birth register and
alphabetical index for **1929**, and search for **Penina / Perl Kahan or Kahana**, later **Miller**,
daughter of **Chaim Arye / Leib / Leb Kahan (born 10 April 1903, Máragyulafalu/Giulești)** and **Leah**.
The birthplace is supplied by her nephew; the exact birth date is unknown. Please also check the **1930**
birth register/index for her brother **Dov / Bernard Kahana**, with the same parents. Please confirm
any civil-name variants from the record rather than assuming them. For a match, provide the full original
entry with headings and marginal annotations, the entry number, page, book reference, and any applicable
access requirements or fees before proceeding. The online second-copy series examined ends with volume
MM-F-00743-1-1-00650 (1918–1920); please check the first-copy series or the municipal holdings for later years.”

The [municipal civil-registration service](https://www.arhiva.primaria-sighet.ro/subpagina/serviciul-stare-civila-se-ocupa-de)
is an identified alternative inquiry route, but custody of these two specific years is not yet confirmed.
No correspondence, paid request, or access application was submitted. The original 1929 birth entry remains unlocated.


## Moses, David Hers and Dov/Bernat: sibling-link audit — 15 October 2026

**The evidence does not establish all three men as brothers with high certainty.** The tree inherited
these common-father links from WikiTree. They have different evidentiary strengths, and no source inspected
in this audit explicitly calls Moses or David Hers a brother of Dov/Bernat. Shared surnames and a linked
WikiTree father are not independent proof.

| Person | Evidence for the proposed father Chaim Arye Leb | Assessment |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Moses Laser Moldovan Kahan, estimated born c.1847** | Original 1848 Gyulafalva census, household 6: **Leb Moldovan**, 22, born Bréb; wife 22; **Mojse**, 1, listed under their children. Film **719825**, DGS **007953483**, **image 9**, household members 1–3. The saved original was re-inspected visually in this audit. | Direct evidence that this Leb had a son Mojse; identification of that infant with the later adult Moses Laser Kahan remains an inference. It does not connect him directly to Dov/Bernat. |
| **David Hers Moldavan Kahan, estimated born c.1855** | His WikiTree biography expressly says no direct record was found establishing him as Chaim Leb's son. Its argument is that the local Kahan families descend from Chaim Leb, with additional support from naming his first son Baruch/Josef. His children's entries document David Hers and Reize, not David's own parents. | A proposed parentage, not established. A family-wide surname hypothesis and naming pattern cannot independently establish the relationship. |
| **Dov Ber Bernat Kahan, estimated born c.1868** | JOWBR **J_MARAMURE_0008659** indexes Dov son of Chaim Aryeh HaKohen, age 56, Giulesti, R.Ch. Shevat 5688; surname is blank and no image is available there. The previously audited Junger relationships provide an indirect bridge to Chaim Leb's family. | Supported indirectly, still unproved: the grave must be linked to this Bernat and its Chaim Aryeh to this particular Chaim Leb. The existing audit details the unresolved identities and age conflicts. |

**Original census:** [FamilySearch image 9](https://www.familysearch.org/ark:/61903/3:1:3Q9M-CS84-X9QK-Y),
re-inspected from the saved original `/tmp/gyulafalva-1848-leb-moldovan.jpg`.
Its child heading is Hungarian **gyermekei**. The wife's name was not resolved here.

**Moses's later adult identification:** [WikiTree Moldovan_Kahan-2](https://www.wikitree.com/wiki/Moldovan_Kahan-2)
cites son Efrajem's birth, **6 March 1869, Book 1098, 08-02**, father **Mose Moldevan**, mother **Reisze**,
godfather **Leb Moldovan**. Later entries use Moses/Laser and Kahan/Moldawan, including Jakob's birth,
**21 July 1874**, and Jaszef Burech's birth, **20 July 1886, Book 1100, 14-03**, surname Katcz/Kahan.
These support an adult household/name-variant argument; a godfather is not automatically a grandfather.
These later entries were read as citations in the current WikiTree biography, not as newly inspected originals.

**David's cited records:** [WikiTree Moldavan_Kahan-1](https://www.wikitree.com/wiki/Moldavan_Kahan-1)
cites Baruch/Josef's birth, **18 March 1875, Book 1098, 09-01**, parents **David/Hirsch Molowan and Reze**;
Abraham's birth, **6 March 1880, Book 1099, 51-01**, father **David/Hers Kahan**;
and Abraham's death, **20 July 1882, Book 1106, 32-10**, father **David/Hers Moldawan**.
They support David's own household and alternating surnames. They do not name his father.
The profile's c.1855 birth estimate is based on the first recorded child in 1875, not a located birth entry.

**Implication for sibling claims:** Moses and Dov would share a father only if Dov's proposed father is
confirmed and the Moses identity chain holds. David and Dov additionally require proof of David's father.
Even if common paternity is confirmed, full rather than paternal half-sibling status requires evidence of
both mothers. Freide Klein cannot be assigned as their common mother from the father's later marriage.

**Tree correction:** David Hers and Dov/Bernat now use the existing **possible** visual marker, with the
uncertainty explained in their notes. Moses retains his census-backed link with the infant/adult identity
limit made explicit. Moses and David's displayed birth years are marked approximate. No people or descendants
were removed and no new parents or mothers were assigned.

**Coverage:** fetched the current WikiTree biographies and father/mother fields through the public profile
API for **Moldovan_Kahan-2, Moldavan_Kahan-1, Moldovan_Kahan-1, Kahan-376**; re-inspected the saved original
1848 census and the earlier Dov parentage audit. The ordinary WikiTree web reader failed, while the public
API succeeded. WikiTree's database father links themselves are not counted as additional sources.

**Next decisive originals:** a marriage, death or birth record for David Hers naming his father; for
Dov/Bernat, his own parental record or a legible gravestone plus evidence resolving both identities;
for Moses, an adult record naming his father or another documented link to the 1848 child. No correspondence
was sent and no fee incurred.


## Chaim Arye Leb: early wife and later Freide Klein — 15 October 2026

**An early wife is directly documented in the 1848 census. The evidence points to two wives, but an exact
lifetime marriage count and the transition between them have not been proved by marriage/death records.**
The earlier tree displayed only Freide Klein and therefore omitted the wife recorded in 1848.

[Original 1848 census, Gyulafalva household 6, image 9](https://www.familysearch.org/ark:/61903/3:1:3Q9M-CS84-X9QK-Y),
film **719825**, DGS **007953483**, shows **Leb Moldovan, age 22; his wife, age 22; child Mojse, age 1**.
The wife's age implies birth about **1826**, approximately. Her name has not been resolved from the image.
She is now listed in the tree as **“Wife recorded in 1848 (name unresolved; age 22)”**, with the census link.
No invented name, marriage date or death date was assigned.

The later birth citations name the mother **Freide**. Her surname **Klein** is supplied by Majer Jakob's
retrospective death registration **1902-229**, Inventory 1373, Book 41, and Ruchel's marriage
**14 August 1901, entry 1901-26**, Inventory 1373, Bárdfalu. These citations were compared in the current
[Chaim biography](https://www.wikitree.com/wiki/Moldovan_Kahan-1) and
[Freide biography](https://www.wikitree.com/wiki/Klein-9716); they are index transcriptions, not originals
newly inspected in this pass.

| Child | Birth citation | Mother named | Chaim's age using the listed birth date 24 June 1827 |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Fischel | 28 December 1867; Book 1098, **08-01** | Freide | 40 |
| Riwke | 9 September 1874; Book 1098, **09-06** | Freide | 47 |
| Majer Jakob | 30 July 1877; Book 1099, **25-07** | Freide | 50 |
| Ruchel | 29 July 1880; Book 1099, **26-02** | Freide | 53 |
| Frimet | 18 August 1883; Book 1099, **28-03** | Freide | 56 |

These paternal ages explain the apparent long span: the late children do not require a third wife;
their cited records consistently name Freide. WikiTree estimates Freide's birth at **about 1850**, but
its citations do not establish that birth year. On that provisional estimate she would be approximately
27, 30 and 33 at the three last births. The 1848 wife's recorded age would put her at approximately
51, 54 and 57 instead. This supports distinguishing an earlier wife from the later mother, but does not
substitute for an original documenting remarriage. The tree now labels Freide's birth year as uncertain.

Dwora's birth **16 January 1858, Book 1097, 08-07**, gives only a cut-off maternal name **“…ter”**.
It cannot yet identify the 1848 wife or establish a third wife. Dov/Bernat's mother remains unproved;
neither the earlier wife nor Freide is assigned as his mother by this update. David Hers's parentage
remains hypothetical. No additional mother-to-child links were created.

**Next:** read Dwora's original and seek Chaim/Leb's marriage to Freide or the earlier wife's death;
look for records establishing Freide's own age. No correspondence or fees were authorized or incurred.


## Ruchel Kahan and Smil Fogel: marriage citation added — 15 October 2026

**Ruchel married Smil Fogel on 14 August 1901 in Bardfalu, Szigeti, Máramaros.** The existing Ruchel
entry now includes her spouse and the complete citation supplied by Hillel, also verified in the current
[WikiTree Kahan-384 biography](https://www.wikitree.com/wiki/Kahan-384#Sources) through the public API.
The linked spouse is [Smil Fogel, Fogel-302](https://www.wikitree.com/wiki/Fogel-302), identified by that
API response rather than inferred from a name match. No original marriage image was inspected in this pass.

>"Máramaros Vital Records Marriages," database, JewishGen. Groom: FOGEL Smil. Bride: KAHAN Ruchel. Groom DOB: 05-Jan-1875. Bride DOB: 29-Jul-1880. Groom hometown: Alsorona. Bride hometown: Maragyulafalu. Groom father: Leb. Groom mother: FOGEL Prive. Bride father: Chaim Leb. Bride mother: KLEIN Freida. Groom father's hometown: Alsorona. Bride's father's hometown: Maragyulafalu. Bride's father's occupation: farmer. Marriage date: 14-Aug-1901. Marriage town: Bardfalu, Szigeti, Máramaros. Witnesses: MENDELOVICS Lajos, KAHAN Bernad. Comments: "Bride's father deceased. Witness #1 age 43, Kolozsvar; witness #2 age 30, Maragyulafalu.". Citing: Romanian National Archive - Baia Mare Branch - Inventory 1373 - Book, 1901-26.

**What the record establishes as transcribed:** Ruchel's parents are Chaim Leb and Freida Klein;
Smil's parents are Leb and Prive Fogel. Smil's birth date is 5 January 1875 and Ruchel's 29 July 1880.
Chaim Leb is already deceased at the marriage, consistent with the existing 24 June 1897 death citation.
The bride's father's occupation is recorded as farmer. Bernad Kahan's presence as a 30-year-old witness
is not an explicit statement that he is Ruchel's brother. The citation leaves the book number unspecified;
only inventory **1373** and entry **1901-26** are retained.

**Earlier wife's chronology:** the census directly places her alive in **1848**; Freide is named as
mother by **28 December 1867** (Fischel, Book 1098, 08-01). The household transition is therefore
bracketed by those observations. **A death after the 1848 census and before the later union, by 1867,
is only a conditional hypothesis if the earlier marriage ended by widowhood.** Different mothers do
not establish the earlier wife's death; divorce or other explanations are not excluded by the cited records.
No death year or death interval has been entered as an established vital fact. Her tree spouse label now
states “alive 1848,” while Chaim's note records this conditional chronology.

# Dov Ber Bernat Kahan: depth-first descendant investigation

Started 15 October 2026. **In progress; not a completed investigation.**
Baseline: GitHub commit `c185c11`, 52 named people in this descendant branch; 119 named people in the full tree.
Private-child counts are not named people. The starting records are preserved locally separately from changes.

## Method and current frontier

Research Dov, then Jente, then each of her descendant branches before returning to Brana and the other siblings.
Newly validated children extend that branch before the next sibling. Spouses support identity and relationship
checks; unrelated spouse ancestry is not recursively expanded. No fees or correspondence are authorized.

Current stack: **saved at unresolved originals/private-family frontier**. Jente’s named descendant branches have a first-pass review;
unidentified private relatives and original-record requests remain open. Brana/Hane index review is recorded below.
Resolved lead: Pearl Jessica’s notice and the matching Montreal family memorials establish Lipot’s two children.
New named descendants so far: nine supported by notices, official burial records or original testimony,
plus one provisional Geni-only child (ten additions).
JewishGen authenticated access restored by the user at 10:29 on 15 October 2026.

## Skill update

Installed and validated a depth-first descendant workflow in the existing jewish-genealogy-research skill,
with source-family coverage, visited identities, a resumable frontier and per-person change summaries.
The naming guidance distinguishes a common Ashkenazi custom from an absolute rule. It also guards against
inferring death from remarriage, a birthplace from residence, or parentage from shared surnames.
Naming sources: [YIVO](https://encyclopedia.yivo.org/article/2126),
[JewishGen naming traditions](https://www.jewishgen.org/infofiles/givennames/slide7.html).

## Findings and per-person summaries

Entries below are added as research is performed. Unreviewed rows above are not negative searches.

### Dov / Jente / Lipot: first evidence checkpoint, 15 October 2026

**New relationship evidence, not a newly discovered birth:** the authenticated Máramaros births search reproduces
Jente, 7 February 1894, father KAHAN Bernat and mother LIEBERMAN Rifke, both Gyulafalu; Book 1101, 37-01.
Lipot’s [photographed Holocaust family memorial](https://images.findagrave.com/photos/2023/176/131335147_24c45f38-da7d-4ceb-9ec4-dab019c3aa0f.jpeg)
was visually inspected. The decisive mother line reads **אמו אלטע יענטע בת ר בערל** — his mother Alta Yenta,
daughter of Reb Berl. The father is Yaakov, son of Liber. The family is commemorated as murdered in Auschwitz in
1944. This materially supports the existing Jente-to-Bernat/Dov Ber connection, together with the birth index and
Be’er Chaim memorial already in the baseline. The linked [Geni Jente](https://www.geni.com/people/Jente-Lieberman/6000000016589578823)
instead names Avraham as father. That contrary tree assertion is preserved; no original naming Avraham was found
in this check. The father’s given name Berl alone would not identify a person without the connected household.
The memorial’s collective mention of murdered siblings cannot mean every sibling died: Naftali survived.
Do not identify unnamed siblings or assign individual death places from that collective sentence alone.

**New descendants:** Lipot’s [memorial 131335147](https://www.findagrave.com/memorial/131335147/lipot-lieberman)
links wife Chaja Ganz (1924–2011), son Jack Samuel (1946–1996) and daughter Pearl Jessica (died 2026).
[Pearl’s original Paperman notice](https://www.paperman.com/funerals/Pearl-Jessica-Lieberman-BF820F71)
independently identifies parents Lipot/Chaja and brother Jack. Her death was 20 January 2026, funeral 22 January,
Hebrew Sick Benefit Association Cemetery, de la Savane, Montreal. Added Pearl and Jack below the existing Lipot.
No birth date or absence of children is inferred for Pearl.

[Jack’s memorial 131335145](https://www.findagrave.com/memorial/131335145/jack_samuel-lieberman)
gives birth 10 December 1946, Romania; death 20 January 1996, Saint-Laurent; burial 21 January, Baron de Hirsch,
Hungarian Hebrew B22, line 2 grave 48. Its transcribed inscription calls him Yaakov Shmuel son of Aryeh.
Wife [Devorah “Denise” Gloutney, memorial 291763558](https://www.findagrave.com/memorial/291763558/devorah-lieberman)
was born 21 June 1942, died 4 October 2002; burial 6 October, Kehal Israel, Congregation Beit Abraham extension
3C grave 39. These two inscriptions are index transcriptions here; only Lipot’s family footstone was visually read.

[Chaja’s memorial 131334973](https://www.findagrave.com/memorial/131334973/chaja-lieberman)
gives birth 26 October 1924, Romania; death 20 September 2011; burial 21 September, Baron de Hirsch B22,
line 5 grave 23. It reproduces a 2011 obituary naming children Pearl and Jack, grandchildren Carl, Michael and
Juanita with spouses Leah and Yitz, and five great-grandchildren Aryeh, Ishai, Aliya, Neve and Cole.
Pearl’s original notice corroborates the niece/nephew generation. The precise parentage within that generation
is still under review. These names are leads, not permission to assign all five great-grandchildren to one couple.
[Young Israel of West Hartford’s bulletin, 24 September 2011](https://images.shulcloud.com/628/uploads/Bulletins/yiwh-bulletin-2011-09-24.pdf)
independently identifies Juanita Moss as Chaja’s granddaughter. The five given names are not yet tree nodes.

**Source coverage and limits:** WikiTree Jente/Lipot/Naftali bios and citations read; linked Geni Jente inspected;
Máramaros births surname LIEBERMAN phonetic, **410 entries, all 9 pages** checked for Kahan/Gyulafalu households;
marriages same surname/mode, **41 entries, entire result** inspected. No Jente–Jakov marriage or 1914/1919 child
birth was identified in those results. Other Jente Kahan mothers with Ajzik Lieberman, active by 1895, are distinct
from a child born in 1894. The requested second town filter did not survive in the result heading, so this was a
surname-wide search. Births/deaths elsewhere, unindexed later civil books and restricted Canadian records remain
gaps; this is not a claim that those records do not exist. Migration evidence and death dates were already in the
baseline. Lipot’s indexed 1949 age 34 is one year short of the January 1914 birth: original passenger list needed.

**Citation defect:** Naftali’s WikiTree JOWBR URL ending `J_QC_0000775` currently returns Leah Silverstein,
died 9 August 1986. It does not verify Naftali’s burial. The tree now labels that citation problem; the burial itself
has not been disproved. Search for the corrected LIBERMAN Naftali, 5 October 1989, Laval/Lubavitch entry continues.

**Next decisive originals:** Jente/Jakob marriage, approximately 1912–1914, Giulești/Berbești registration district,
with bride’s parents; Lipot birth 20 January 1914; Naftali birth 23 October 1919. Request book identification and
entry images from Baia Mare/Maramureș project if the catalog does not expose them. No request has been sent.

### Lieberman branch: resolved citation and additional generation

The complete Quebec JOWBR query (LIBERMAN, phonetic, 186 results, all four pages) locates
[Naftali’s correct entry J_QC_0079881](https://www.jewishgen.org/databases/cemetery/jowbr.php?rec=J_QC_0079881).
The [original photograph](https://data.jewishgen.org/imagedata/jowbr/CAN-04593/duvernay_lub39.jpg)
was visually inspected: Naftali son of Yaakov; his mother’s name Alta Yenta; died 6 Tishrei 5750.
The index pairs this with 5 October 1989 and birth 23 October 1919. Burial location is confirmed.
No named descendants were identified; the cited single status and “dear uncle” wording do not prove childlessness.
Lipot’s correct entry is [J_QC_0045188](https://www.jewishgen.org/databases/cemetery/jowbr.php?rec=J_QC_0045188),
Chaja [J_QC_0045186](https://www.jewishgen.org/databases/cemetery/jowbr.php?rec=J_QC_0045186),
and Jack [J_QC_0045056](https://www.jewishgen.org/databases/cemetery/jowbr.php?rec=J_QC_0045056).
[Jack’s original grave photo](https://data.jewishgen.org/imagedata/jowbr/CAN-01691/B22_240.jpg)
was now also inspected: Yaakov Shmuel son of Aryeh; civil dates match.

Added **Carl Lieberman, Michael Lieberman and Juanita Lieberman (Moss)** under Jack with the indirect argument
explicit in each note: Chaja’s obituary identifies her children Pearl/Jack and these three grandchildren;
Pearl’s notice identifies the same three as nieces/nephews. This supports Jack as father. The individual mother
has not been independently established, so Denise’s spouse entry must not be treated as proof of maternity.
Spouses Leah Lieberman and Yitz Moss come from the notices; further identity details are not inferred.
Targeted web searches combining those names with family names identified the notices and Juanita’s synagogue
bulletin, but did not establish additional parent-child links. Five great-grandchildren remain in the ledger
without parents assigned. Romanian/Hungarian birth collections do not cover these later Canadian generations;
private Geni and restricted vital records are unresolved routes, not negative results.

**Provisional additional child of Jente:** [Frida/Freide Lieberman, Geni 6000000016589686765](https://www.geni.com/people/Frida-Freide-Lieberman/6000000016589686765),
1917–c.1944, Romania. Her profile identifies father Jacov/Jankel; Jente’s timeline lists her birth. Added with the
existing tree’s uncertain-link marker and explicit secondary-source warning. No named spouse or children were
visible. Geni shows four children for Jente; three are named, leaving one unidentified private child. It also shows
two private children for Lipot, consistent with the two children independently named in public notices; they have
not been duplicated as placeholders. Frida’s individual original, precise birth/death and descendants remain open.

### Regional index coverage for the following siblings

Máramaros datasets, town exact queries, all pages: **GYULAFALVA** births 65, deaths 2, marriages 1;
**GYULAFALU** births 224, deaths 69, marriages 22; **MARAGYULAFALU** births 57, deaths 24, marriages 5.
These result sets overlap and must not be summed as unique people. They reconfirm Brana’s 1896 birth/1898 death,
Hane’s 1900 birth, Ziszel’s 1902 birth and 1933 marriage annotation, Leb’s 1903 birth, Majer Jakab’s 1905 birth
and 1937 marriage annotation. They do not reach all later civil years. A surname-exact KAHAN query in the Romanian
returning-survivor collection returned 50 records, all inspected; no target household identified. The Maramureș
JOWBR surname-exact query returned 17 records, all inspected; a separate town-exact **GIULESTI** query returned
11 entries including unnamed-surname Dov son of Chaim Aryeh HaKohen (0008659). That grave remains a candidate
for Bernat, not proof of which Chaim Aryeh was his father. No original photograph is supplied for that entry.

A collateral entry deserves follow-up without merging families: Mojse KAHAN, age 7, son of Abraham KAHAN and
Feige LAXNER, died 23 May 1903, Maragyulafalu, Inventory 1373 entry **1903-81**; informant Bernad KAHAN is called
his uncle. This is stronger than surname coincidence but an uncle can be maternal or by marriage. The original
and the parents’ marriage are required before assigning Abraham as Bernat’s brother.

### Ziszel: new Israeli burial candidate

Israel JOWBR surname MUSKAL, phonetic, 126 results, all three pages inspected.
[Record J_ISRAEL_bg1393575](https://www.jewishgen.org/databases/notglue_s2.php?rec=J_ISRAEL_bg1393575)
identifies Zisl/Zissel/Zisele Muskal, died 18 July 1969 (3 Av 5729), father **Dov HaKohen**, Hof HaCarmel, Haifa.
The linked [BillionGraves record 29323054](https://billiongraves.com/grave/זיסל-מוסקל/29323054) repeats this information.
These are one source chain, not independent confirmation. The public thumbnail was inspected but cannot be read
reliably; full-resolution access was offered separately and no paid access was used. This advances the baseline’s
unspecified candidate substantially but is not yet conclusive identification: no age, husband or birthplace given.
The public Geni Zisel profile reconfirms husband Yoachim and no named children; that does not prove childlessness.
The official Haifa burial search is the next independent check.

### Ziszel / new son Dov Muskal: official records and photographs

The [official Haifa surname search](https://kdh.org.il/regular-serch/search-results-deceased/?general_text=מוסקל)
returns a row **Dov Muskal — father Yoachim, mother Zisl — died 8 August 2011**. This is a newly identified
child, absent from the starting tree. [Record 102399](https://kdh.org.il/regular-serch/search-results-deceased/grave-info/?deceased=102399)
gives birth **2 November 1936, Romania**, death 9 Av 5771, Tel Regev/Nachalat Binyamin, section י, row 22,
place 54א. The [photographed stone](https://kdh.org.il/wp-content/themes/firma-child/assets/images/pic/picmaz/111492.jpg)
was inspected and explicitly names Chaim and Zisl as parents. It describes a husband, father, brother and
grandfather. Further descendants existed but their names are not supplied; no child or wife has been invented.
The official displayed age 75 differs from **74 completed years** calculated from its own dates. Preserve the
explicit dates while noting this discrepancy. The Romanian original birth and an obituary/family confirmation
naming his wife and children are the next records. Searches for Dov Muskal in Hebrew/English with 2011, Zisl and
family wording did not identify a securely connected family notice. Living namesakes/business profiles are not merged.

[Zisl’s official record 33299](https://kdh.org.il/regular-serch/search-results-deceased/grave-info/?deceased=33299)
independently confirms Romanian birth, father Dov, death **18 July 1969**, Hof HaCarmel section ו, row 6, place 49.
Its [legible original photograph](https://kdh.org.il/wp-content/themes/firma-child/assets/images/pic/picmaz/26639.jpg)
was inspected: Zisl Muskal, daughter of Dov HaKohen, wife and mother. The mother/father pair in the son’s official
row connects the known Yoachim/Zisl couple and supports high-confidence identification with the 1902-born Ziszel.
This is a corroborated identity argument, not an original naming Bernat on her death record.

[Yoachim Chaim’s official record 46151](https://kdh.org.il/regular-serch/search-results-deceased/grave-info/?deceased=46151)
gives birth **1 January 1909, Romania**, death **29 September 1983**, parents Marko/Fania in the search row,
burial Sde Yehoshua section לז, row 12, place 10. His [photographed inscription](https://kdh.org.il/wp-content/themes/firma-child/assets/images/pic/picmaz/51754.jpg)
reads Chaim Muskal, son of Yitzhak Mordechai. The civil/Hebrew father-name pairing requires further corroboration;
no ancestor is attached. January 1 may be an administrative date and has not been presented as inspected birth evidence.
Spouse label now includes the documented death chronology.

**Chronology:** on the son’s recorded birth, Ziszel was 34 and Yoachim approximately 27. This is compatible with
the 1933 marriage. Dov’s 1936 birth after the grandfather’s proposed 1928 death fits commemorative naming, but
neither proves that the grandson was named after him nor independently proves the grandfather’s death date.
The names, parents, spouses and records—not the naming custom—support the new relationship.

### Chaim Arye’s branch: public-profile review and living-family limits

Geni’s Chaim Arye, Penina, Dov (1930), Shalom and Rachel profiles were read through the public people pages.
The 1903 birth original and 1951/1960 arrival originals were already in the baseline and are not counted as new.
Penina still shows three private children; the arrival sheet independently groups two daughters but does not name
a third. Chaim Arye still shows one unidentified child beyond the three named siblings. No additional birth entry
was identified in the inspected Máramaros result sets; these do not cover all 1929–1943 birth registers.
Exact Romanian archive requests for Sighet 1929/1930 and Timișoara children remain in the existing research notes.

Dov’s public Geni profile currently names Chaim-Arie and Aviad within seven child slots, while
[the existing Wikipedia source](https://he.wikipedia.org/wiki/דב_כהנא) reports six children, including Zvi and Meir.
The count and identity overlap are not reconciled. These two names remain ledger candidates; do not turn the
private counts into a supposedly exact total. Geni provides no public profile link for those two names in this view.
Zvi’s and Meir’s known parentage was reconfirmed in the public biographies; no additional named child of Zvi was
established. The [2023 Meir profile](https://www.inn.co.il/news/598377) reconfirms wife Bruria and five children.
Added Bruria to the spouse display; the maiden name Tzuriel is separately a secondary Wikipedia assertion.
No individual child names were established. Cemetery/death records are not a suitable way to identify these living
children without evidence of a death; public family profiles or family confirmation are the next route.

Shalom’s Geni count still conflicts with the user-confirmed fourteen. The family testimony takes precedence;
no new child was added from the larger Geni count. Rachel’s public profile describes death at two weeks in 1972,
consistent with the baseline infant identity. Exact dates remain hidden in this public view. For Haim Arie,
Yael, Yedidia, Nachshon, Elisheva, Ishai, Hillel, Avia, Akiva, Gad, Lea and Iska, the supplied first names do not
identify unique public family profiles or establish their children. No new fact established for these twelve.
A family roster of spouses and children was requested asynchronously; research on archival branches continued.
These are explicit unresolved identity limits, not completed searches of every modern archive or proof of no children.

[Michal’s first-person interview, 4 July 2019](https://www.inn.co.il/news/406366), names her seven children and reports
ages: **Eliav 28, Renana 27, Avital 25, Yitzhak 23, Naomi 20, Shulamit 19, Tamar 12**. The names/relationships were
already in the baseline; the ages are newly recorded in each child’s panel. No exact birthday was calculated.
Individual Hebrew-name searches (with parent names where needed) returned no securely connected descendant record;
unrelated historic rabbis, scholarly material and commercial profiles were not merged. Married surnames remain a gap.
Michal also describes the family’s move to Antwerp and return after her father’s death; this is migration testimony,
not proof that all the children were born in Belgium. No additional named grandchild was established.

### Feige and the Adler children: original ages and source correction

[Original testimony image 216](https://assets.yadvashem.org/image/upload/t_f_image/f_auto/q_auto/v1/remote_media/arch1_yadvashem/12030923_401_0021/216.jpg)
was visually inspected. It is Nathan Yitzhak/Aizik Adler’s sheet, submitted by brother-in-law Menachem Kohen:
wife **Feiga Kohen, age 40**, **seven children**, named **Dov 14, Yehudit 14, Ester 11**. This supports estimated
births c.1930, c.1930 and **c.1933**, respectively. Corrected Ester from the baseline’s c.1930. Four children
remain unidentified. Equal reported ages are not proof of twins; the two sisters Feige/Reiza also do not become
twins merely because both have estimated birth year 1904. Feige’s approximate birth now has an explicit age basis.
The husband’s 1902 testimony birth differs from the 14 February 1904 civil index already in the baseline.

WikiTree’s citation calls [8638643](https://collections.yadvashem.org/en/names/8638643) the father’s record,
but it is **Ester’s derived entry**, age 11, extracted from that same image. The father’s actual entry is
[1859762](https://collections.yadvashem.org/en/names/1859762). These are not independent witnesses.
All three children are already in the baseline; no further descendant or exact child birth/death record established.
A fresh Geni Feige request returned an empty page; that check did not run successfully and is not a negative result.

### Reiza and the Wieder children: three newly named descendants

[Yad Vashem grouped record 13777487](https://collections.yadvashem.org/en/names/13777487) contains two originals,
both inspected at readable resolution: item **811147**, Menachem Kohen, brother-in-law
([image 6](https://assets.yadvashem.org/image/upload/t_f_image/f_auto/q_auto/v1/remote_media/arch1_yadvashem/22021838_249_1215/6.jpg));
and item **671693**, nephew Meir Zikherman
([image 4](https://assets.yadvashem.org/image/upload/t_f_image/f_auto/q_auto/v1/remote_media/arch1_yadvashem/22021838_249_1215/4.jpg)).
Menachem explicitly names wife **Reiza Kohen, daughter of Dov**, and **seven children**. Meir independently names
wife Reiza Kohen and murdered children **Tzipora, David, Yaakov Tzvi, Menachem Mendel**; Menachem also names Rachel.
Added **Tzipora, Yaakov Tzvi and Menachem Mendel**, absent from the baseline. Existing Rakhel, Mirl and David remain,
for six named children and one unidentified child. Mirl is not merged with Menachem based on similar letters.
No individual birth year, death date, death camp or childlessness is inferred for the three additions.
Their names appear in the murdered-children section; the form permits older children too, so the header alone
does not establish that all were under eighteen. Exact original birth entries and individual testimonies remain gaps.

The repeated husband’s parents Yaakov Tzvi/Brana Rivka, Deșești residence and wife Reiza strengthen the connection
to the earlier niece’s testimony that called the wife Rakhel. Husband’s birth estimates **1895, 1896 and 1900**
remain discrepant; no single exact year is selected. This is materially stronger spouse/parentage evidence than the
baseline’s “probable husband” note, and explicitly links Reiza to father Dov. No new parent of Bernat is established.

### Majer Jakab / Dov (1937) / Tzippora: identity and access limits

The authenticated birth index reconfirms Majer Jakab, 21 March 1905, Bernat/Regina, Book 36 entry 1905-72,
with annotation marrying Roza Indig, 27 March 1937, Giulești. This was already in the baseline. The matching wife
and name make the Geni Meir Yakob household a useful lead, but the father “Dov Heller” remains a real conflict.
Removed the baseline’s unsupported suggestion that it was simply a data-entry slip. No evidence of a surname
change to Heller has been found in the records inspected here. No extra wife, parent or sibling is attached.

Geni fresh access later returned empty pages (including Meir’s direct people page), after earlier public people
pages had worked. This is an access failure, not absence of children. The baseline Dov (1937–2019) and Tzippora
(b.1939) relationships, each with four private child slots, remain secondary-tree assertions pending originals or
family confirmation. Exact-name web searches with the known years/maiden names supplied no safely identifiable
new family record. No new descendant established for either. They remain separate from Dov born 1930. The next
step is the children’s originals/family roster and a uniquely matched 2019 burial/obituary; a common name alone
cannot identify a grave or living relative. Roza’s source-indexed survival remains a baseline fact, not proof of
Majer’s survival or death. No Romanian later birth original was retrieved for this household.

### Abraham Lieber / Beniamin / Dov: three unidentified children

The [original Abraham Lieber testimony](https://assets.yadvashem.org/image/upload/t_f_image/f_auto/q_auto/v1/remote_media/arch1_yadvashem/25021218_207_1709/20.jpg)
was re-read. **New to the tree:** the sheet explicitly reports **five children** and names only **Dov and Beniamin**.
Three unnamed children are now counted as unidentified, not assumed living. The father’s Bernat/Dov and mother’s
Regina/Rivka pairing, wife Kreindel Shreiber and Săcueni residence were already known. No age, precise birth date,
spouse or descendants of the two named sons is supplied on this original. It is not five separate birth records.
The [derived Dov entry 8605421](https://collections.yadvashem.org/en/names/8605421) and
[Beniamin entry 8605422](https://collections.yadvashem.org/en/names/8605422) are from that same source chain.
A family census, civil births and additional testimony naming the three children are the next needed records;
no new candidate name was attached. Săcueni’s wartime geography differs from Giulești; the surname does not
justify applying the Giulești register’s gaps to the children’s births there.

### Freide / husband Yitzhak Herskovits: original children field examined

[Husband’s original, item 531116, image 378](https://assets.yadvashem.org/image/upload/t_f_image/f_auto/q_auto/v1/remote_media/arch1_yadvashem/11030919_419_3833/378.jpg)
was inspected: wife **Frida Kohen, age 30**; a dash in the children-count position and a crossed-out children table.
**No child is reported on this sheet.** It does not prove that no child was ever born. Her
[own testimony 837292](https://collections.yadvashem.org/en/names/837292) indexes 1912, parents Berl/Dov and Rifke,
occupation seamstress, husband Yitzkhak; this matches the baseline family. The husband’s age estimate for her
suggests c.1913–1914 and is a minor unresolved discrepancy. The 3 May 1912 Falusugatag claim in an Ancestry tree
has not been verified in an original birth entry. No extra descendants added.

### Shimon Menachem / Frida / Tova Blima: baseline confirmation and naming caveat

The [wife’s original, item 1600529](https://assets.yadvashem.org/image/upload/t_f_image/f_auto/q_auto/v1/remote_media/arch1_yadvashem/16021625_265_0482/180.jpg)
was re-read. It reports two children, **Frida 1940 and Tova Blima 1942**, consistent with the baseline; no new
child established for either. Their young ages do not create evidence for descendants. The wife’s Hebrew given
name is Frimed/Frimet while her civil entry is Frida; the daughter is also called Frida. This is exactly why the
Ashkenazi naming custom cannot be used as an absolute veto: civil names and Hebrew names can differ, and
practice varies. This sheet does not establish that daughter and mother had the same Hebrew name. Its mother
line for the wife includes Alti Chaya Rachel; no unrelated spouse ancestry is recursively added.

The already-verified official Netanya death/burial **2 January 1973**, father Dov, remains authoritative over the
older inconsistent profile citation. Shimon Menachem’s birth-year conflict (c.1900, c.1913, age-based estimates)
remains unresolved; no new birth year is selected. No evidence of another marriage or postwar child was established
in the inspected sources. A later civil marriage/death file or family information is needed to examine that possibility.

## Prepared original-image request (not sent)

To the Máramaros Jewish Records Indexing Project / Romanian National Archives, Baia Mare branch:
Please identify the current catalog references and supply the complete entries, headings and marginal annotations
for the following connected Giulești/Gyulafalu family. Please distinguish birth date from registration date and
identify any parent, spouse or informant omitted from the index. No paid order or correspondence has been placed.

1. Jente KAHAN, born 7 February 1894, Bernat KAHAN and Rifke LIEBERMAN; religious Book 1101, **37-01**.
2. Jente KAHAN and Jakov/Jankel LIBERMAN marriage, approximately 1912–1914, Giulești/Berbești district;
   bride’s father conflicting Bernat/Berl versus Avraham. Groom born 12 December 1892; parents Abraham Liber
   Liberman and Ruchel Lebovics. Please identify the book/entry rather than assume one.
3. Lipot LIBERMAN birth, 20 January 1914; Naftali LIBERMAN birth, 23 October 1919; and possible Frida/Freide
   LIBERMAN birth in 1917, parents Jakov/Jankel and Jente/Alta Yenta. Book/entry not yet identified.
4. Ziszel KAHAN, 24 February 1902, Inventory 1373 Book 35, **1902-45**, including the annotation of marriage
   to Joachim MUSCAL on 19 October 1933; the 1933 marriage entry itself; Dov MUSKAL birth 2 November 1936,
   parents Yoachim/Chaim and Zisl. Birth town within Romania not established for the son.
5. Majer Jakab KAHAN, 21 March 1905, Inventory 1373 Book 36, **1905-72**, including the 27 March 1937
   Roza INDIG marriage annotation; original marriage to distinguish Bernat Kahan from the Geni Dov Heller claim.
6. Mojse KAHAN death, 23 May 1903, Maragyulafalu, Inventory 1373, **1903-81**, parents Abraham Kahan and
   Feige Laxner; exact wording describing informant Bernad Kahan as uncle. Book number unspecified in the index.

Separate Israeli/Canadian family requests: the wife/children of Dov Muskal (1936–2011); spouses/children for
Shalom and Ruth’s confirmed fourteen; identification of Penina’s three private children and the Majer branch’s
private children; the parents of Chaja Lieberman’s named great-grandchildren. These requests are research gaps,
not missing relationships filled by guessing. No message has been sent to any third party.
