## Dov Muskal record 102399 reinspection — 15 October 2026

User-directed source review; the family interview remains paused at Renana’s children.
**Result: the supplied record confirms the existing Dov Muskal entry; it adds no new named relative.**
This person was already added in the earlier research pass, rather than discovered anew from this link.

- [Official Haifa burial record 102399](https://kdh.org.il/regular-serch/search-results-deceased/grave-info/?deceased=102399):
  **דב מוסקל**, father **יואכים**, born **2 November 1936, Romania**, died **8 August 2011 / 9 Av 5771**;
  Nachalat Binyamin–Tel Regev, section **י**, row **22**, place **54א**. It displays age **75**; the dates yield
  **74 completed years**, which remains an explicit discrepancy. This is retrospective burial-register birth data,
  not an inspected Romanian birth entry. No exact birth town is supplied.
- The [official surname-search row](https://kdh.org.il/regular-serch/search-results-deceased/?general_text=מוסקל)
  independently exposes the page’s parent columns: father **יואכים**, mother **זיסל**, death **08/08/2011**.
  The individual detail page itself shows only the father. Both views belong to the same registry, not two independent sources.
- [Original gravestone photograph 111492.jpg](https://kdh.org.il/wp-content/themes/firma-child/assets/images/pic/picmaz/111492.jpg),
  visually re-read at full available resolution. Decisive transcription (line breaks retained):

  > בעלי אבינו אחי וסבנו היקר<br>
  > דב מוסקל<br>
  > בן חיים וזיסל ז״ל

  Translation: “My dear husband, our father, my brother and our grandfather; Dov Muskal; son of Chaim and Zisl, of blessed memory.”
  The following death line reads **ט׳ באב תשע״א**. The page and stone connect the civil father name Yoachim
  with the Hebrew name Chaim through the same son and mother; neither name alone identifies an unrelated person.
  The familial titles establish a spouse, child or children, sibling or siblings, and grandchild or grandchildren,
  but provide **no individual names, sex, count, or birth dates** for those relatives. No placeholder people were invented.

**Identity and limits:** existing maternal-line placement is retained: Dov Ber/Bernat Kahan → Ziszel Kahan (Muscal)
→ Dov Muskal. The earlier report already explains the connection through Ziszel’s 1902 birth/marriage annotation,
her Haifa burial and her son’s parent pair. This reinspection does not establish Dov Ber’s own mother or grandparents,
nor identify Dov Muskal’s wife, children or exact Romanian birth town.

**Search coverage:** the official מוסקל surname search displayed 22 matches. Page 1 showed 20 rows, and the Next control
loaded page 2 with three rows overlapping page 1; therefore the displayed total was not treated as 22 distinct inspected
identities. The verified Dov, Zisl and Yoachim rows were on page 1. Open-web queries used דב מוסקל / דוב מוסקל,
Dov Muskal / Dov Muscal / Dov Muszkal, with 2011, 8.8.2011, Zisl, Joachim, Haifa, family/death terms,
and site restrictions to avelim.co.il and geni.com. No returned item securely named this man’s wife or descendants.
A currently active professional profile with the same name is not evidence about this man, who died in 2011.
A surname-only telephone-directory hit likewise does not establish identity or kinship. These searches are bounded
results, not proof that no obituary or relatives exist. The web reader failed to open the official page; the connected
browser successfully loaded the detail and search pages, and the original photograph was downloaded and visually inspected.

**Next decisive evidence:** a family identification or obituary naming Dov’s wife/children, followed by records explicitly
linking them; the Romanian birth entry of 2 November 1936 once the birth town is established. No correspondence was sent
and no fees were incurred. No names or dates were changed by this reinspection; the tree now records its evidence audit.


## Dov Muskal: ship arrival found; wife and children search — 15 October 2026

**New original evidence:** Israel State Archives, **GL-15505/14**, file **0009l8z**, *Immigrant lists,
February–March 1965*. Pinpoint document **a251147b0dccc0e1_585158a3cc8e02af**, **scan 5 of 175, printed page 2,
rows 66–69**. The heading on **scan 4, printed page 1** identifies arrivals aboard **ספיר (Sapir), 2 February 1965**.
Both scans were downloaded and visually inspected; the result is not based solely on OCR.

| Row | Name as read | Birth year | Other information |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 66 | מוסקל, יואכים (Yoachim Muskal; given-name spelling indistinct in places) | 1909 | Father Marko; origin Romania; immigrant number 242407; destination ק. בנימין (Kiryat Binyamin), left-column number 3556. |
| 67 | זיסל (Zisl) | 1902 | Grouped immediately below Yoachim; common household fields left blank. |
| 68 | מרקו (Marco) | 1934 | Same household group; no separate parents named. |
| 69 | ברנרדו (Bernardo) | 1936 | Same household group; no separate parents named. |

[Archival viewer](https://journaliststudio.google.com/pinpoint/document-view?collection=585158a3cc8e02af&docid=a251147b0dccc0e1_585158a3cc8e02af&dapvm=2) ·
[original scan 5](https://journaliststudio.google.com/backlight/serve/image?document_id=a251147b0dccc0e1_585158a3cc8e02af&page_number=4&width=800) ·
[preserved scan 5](muskal-1965-arrival-5.png) · [preserved heading, scan 4](muskal-1965-arrival-4.png).

**Identity argument:** Yoachim/Chaim born 1909 and Zisl born 1902 match the already identified parents;
Bernardo’s 1936 birth matches Dov’s official burial birth year. The combined household is a **high-confidence match**
to the family, and Bernardo is very likely Dov’s civil given name. This is a supported indirect identification,
not an explicit name-change certificate. No exact birth date or town is supplied here. The list records Romania as
origin and the ship/date of arrival; it does not by itself prove the ship’s embarkation port, complete route or that
this was anyone’s first visit to Israel. No wife or child is named alongside Bernardo; this does not prove he was unmarried.

**New secondary family information:** the [MyHeritage Dov summary](https://www.myheritage.hu/names/%D7%93%D7%91_%D7%9E%D7%95%D7%A1%D7%A7%D7%9C),
entry **1936–2011**, explicitly reports an unnamed wife and **two children**, and names brother **Marco Mordechai Yitzhak**.
The adjacent BillionGraves summary gives parents Chaim/Zisl and Tel Regev burial, supporting selection of the correct
Dov entry among several namesakes. The underlying tree detail opened a subscription offer; no trial, order or payment
was submitted. Its tree owner and supporting family sources were not verified. The child count is therefore recorded
in the tree as **secondary and unverified**, with no invented child nodes or spouse name. The grave’s father/grandfather
wording supports descendants generally, but does not independently verify the count of two.

The [Marco summary](https://www.myheritage.hu/names/%D7%9E%D7%A8%D7%A7%D7%95_%D7%9E%D7%95%D7%A1%D7%A7%D7%9C)
reports **1934–2013**, wife **Esther Betty née Schlesinger**, and three children. A linked-source summary associates
Mordechai Muskal with father Chaim HaLevi and Tsur Shalom cemetery, death around 25–26 December 2013. These are
**Marco’s candidate family details, not Dov’s wife or children**. Automated translation visibly distorts some names;
the summary’s surname variants are not adopted as historical surname changes. The original Marco stone and underlying
tree have not yet been verified. The 1934 ship passenger plus the explicit secondary brother link make him a probable
brother; he remains in this ledger rather than being attached with unproved maternal parentage.

| Person | New versus prior tree | Confidence / next evidence |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Dov Muskal | 2 Feb 1965 Sapir arrival, likely civil name Bernardo; secondary claim of two unnamed children | High-confidence arrival identity; wife and children’s names remain unresolved. Seek family obituary or an explicitly linked family record. |
| Ziszel Kahan (Muscal) | 2 Feb 1965 arrival with Yoachim and two younger household members | High-confidence household match; original list groups people rather than labeling each relationship. |
| Yoachim/Chaim Muskal | Arrival year/date/ship, 1909 birth reconfirmed, father Marko in arrival row | Existing spouse; not newly discovered. Father-name pairing was already in burial evidence. |
| Marco (1934 passenger) | Newly found household member and probable brother; secondary 1934–2013 candidate | Not added as an established child. Verify original stone, parents and birth before attaching his family. |
| Dov’s wife and two children | Secondary unnamed household only | No individual names established; no further DFS identities invented. |

**Search coverage and limits:**

- State Archives Pinpoint, exact **"מוסקל"**: **25 documents**, all result summaries reviewed; the 1965 parents/year
  match led to inspected scans 4–5. A second document-level query **"ברנרדו" "מוסקל"** returned 11 documents;
  words appearing anywhere in a long file do not establish that they belong to one person.
- [Avelim surname search](https://avelim.co.il/?s=%D7%9E%D7%95%D7%A1%D7%A7%D7%9C) loaded successfully in the browser:
  five visible notices (Ilana wife of Shlomo, Moti, Mordechai, Shmuel, Nechama Ginzburg) supplied no securely linked
  Dov family record. This is a bounded website result, not proof that no obituary exists.
- [Geni Yoachim](https://www.geni.com/people/Yoachim-Muscal/6000000174916954835) and
  [Zisel](https://www.geni.com/people/Zisel-Muscal/6000000016806797537) loaded successfully after a delay. They show
  the couple but no named children in the public view. Earlier blank responses must not be treated as missing profiles.
- Authenticated JewishGen: **Registries of Romanian Survivors Returning from the Shoah**, MUSKAL phonetic surname
  query through the Hungary collection form, returned two rows, both inspected: Mendel MUSCAL, born 18 Dec 1904,
  Vișeu de Sus, Book I M-21; Alexandru MUSCAL, born 18 Oct 1924, Sighet, Book I M-29, mother Regina HUK?. Neither
  identifies this household; no kinship inferred. [Collection description](https://jewishgen.org/databases/holocaust/RomanianSurvivors.html).
- Authenticated JewishGen: **Hungarian Speaking Families Who Wanted to Immigrate to Palestine**, same surname/mode,
  returned zero indexed matches. Its [description](https://www.jewishgen.org/databases/Holocaust/HungarianSpeakersEmigratingtoPalestine.html)
  dates the lists to **1944**, so this is not a negative search of 1965 passenger arrivals.
- New open-web searches added Bernardo/ברנרדו, Marco/מרקו, Bernard/ברנרד, Kiryat Ata/Binyamin and spouse/children terms.
  No securely identified wife or named child of Dov emerged. No claim of exhaustive coverage of all Jewish archives.

**Next exact request, prepared only:** ask the State Archives about the immigrant/registration file linked to
**Yoachim Muskal, 1909, immigrant no. 242407; Zisl 1902; Marco 1934; Bernardo 1936; Sapir, 2 February 1965;
GL-15505/14, rows 66–69**, including any family-relationship or later name fields available for research.
No request was sent. For the wife and children, the most useful next lead is the underlying MyHeritage family tree
or a family-supplied name, then corroboration in a marriage, obituary or family record. Original Romanian 1934/1936 births
still require a verified town. No correspondence, membership request, subscription or fees were authorized or submitted.
The family-interview frontier remains Renana’s children; this was a user-directed detour. Full DFS remains incomplete.

### Newly established: Jack Samuel Lieberman's marriage

The [original Quebec marriage image](https://www.myheritage.co.il/research/collection-10723/quebec-marriage-returns-1926-1997?itemId=1260246-&action=showRecord), collection 10723, record 1260246-, Form E,
certificate **145146** (index **upd68-145146**), was visually inspected. It names **Jack Samuel Lieberman** and
**Marie Denise Therese Gloutney (DEVORAH)**, married **6 November 1968, Montreal**. His birth date is
**10 December 1946**, hers **21 June 1942**. His birthplace is **Rumania**, hers Canada.
This adds an original marriage date, the bride's full civil name and precise birth date; Jack's birth date,
Romanian origin, spouse identity and 1996 death were already in the baseline. MyHeritage's marriage index incorrectly
labels his birthplace Quebec; the original takes precedence. The tree and Hebrew note now distinguish that error.

The [Quebec civil death index](https://www.myheritage.co.il/research/collection-20872/canada-quebec-civil-deaths-1926-1997?itemId=1330643-&action=showRecord), collection 20872, record 1330643-, independently names father
**Lipot Lieberman**, mother **Chaja**, birth 10 December 1946 and death **20 January 1996, Saint-Laurent, Montreal**.
This is index-only evidence; its **Celibataire** marital-status field conflicts with the inspected marriage.
A divorce, later union or index error has not been established. No new child or maternal assignment was made.
Historical street addresses and the full marriage image were not republished.

The Greenblatt tree (updated 11 October 2026) assigns [Penina](https://www.myheritage.co.il/research/collection-1/myheritage-family-trees?itemId=OYYV72YXAE6LNNBPEVZYHI46XSR3EFA-1-523075&action=showRecord), [Dov](https://www.myheritage.co.il/research/collection-1/myheritage-family-trees?itemId=OYYV72YXAE6LNNBPEVZYHI46XSR3EFA-1-523076&action=showRecord) and
[Shalom](https://www.myheritage.co.il/research/collection-1/myheritage-family-trees?itemId=OYYV72YXAE6LNNBPEVZYHI46XSR3EFA-1-509440&action=showRecord) to Kalonymos Kalman Kahana (1816–1899) and Rivka Hartstein-Rappoport (1827–1870).
All three full records were read. The alleged parents died decades before these children's 1929, 1930 and 1943 births.
This proposed ancestry is rejected, and the existing Chaim Arye / Leah parentage remains. These are three profiles
in one erroneous member tree, not three independent confirmations. Their additional precise dates/places remain
unverified claims. Four existing tree profiles received source/evidence updates; named node count remains 138.

### Unattached Moses candidates and original survivor list

- [Mozes Kahan, Gyulafalva, Pinkas HaNitzolim I](https://www.myheritage.co.il/research/collection-20020/pinkas-hanitzolim-volume-i-register-of-jewish-survivors-from-jewishgen?itemId=9498-&action=showRecord), collection 20020, record 9498-:
  MyHeritage labels the list 4C. Following this source led to the [USHMM scan of the original book](https://www.ushmm.org/media/images/vlpnamelist/AO235/AO235.pdf).
  **PDF page 44, left-hand printed page 52, third column**, has **“Kahán Mózes, Gyulafalva”**, between
  the Berehovo and Hernicse namesakes. That line gives no age, parents, spouse or street address.
  Original wording is confirmed, but identity within this family remains unresolved; it does not justify changing
  any 1944 death or attaching a survivor to the tree. The [JewishGen collection guide](https://www.jewishgen.org/databases/Holocaust/0064_PinkasNitzolimI.html)
  maps 4C to printed pages 57–70; that differs from this scan's printed page 52. The index/list designation needs
  reconciliation; no exact sublist date is assigned to this entry here.
- [Moses Leizor Kahan, member tree](https://www.myheritage.co.il/research/collection-1/myheritage-family-trees?itemId=OYYV72J5M5PDPKDK2VPFRXUOKJUAZRY-9-171&action=showRecord):
  birth 1851, parents Chaim Leib / Chaje Taube Perl, wife Ciupa Kudisch, recorded marriage 16 April 1912,
  children including Jakob born **9 March 1887, Stanislawow**. Parent and Jakob records were opened.
  Our Moses has a different documented child chronology, including Jacob born **21 July 1874**. Identity is not
  established; Chaje Taube is not assigned as our Chaim's earlier wife. “Węgry” can denote Hungary and a modern
  Polish geocoding label alone does not disprove identity; a late marriage can also be late registration.
- [Lazer Josef Moldovan, c.1845](https://www.myheritage.co.il/research/collection-1/myheritage-family-trees?itemId=OYYV73EAWJVPJVTHFBAJG3GK6RKBDIQ-1-500184&action=showRecord),
  Moldwin tree: father Moses Itzig c.1813, mother Sure Sarah, wife Rivka, children Salamon 1868 and Abraham 1871.
  This is a separate candidate, not an identified alternate name for our Moses Laser.
- [Giza Moldovan passenger index](https://www.myheritage.co.il/research/collection-10512/ellis-island-other-new-york-passenger-lists-1820-1957?itemId=60026210-F&action=showRecord):
  age 21, arrived 2 December 1913 on Kaiserin Auguste Victoria from Hamburg, father Moses Moldovan,
  last residence Marmaroszcges; NARA T715, roll 2231, RG85, row 20, viewer image 154/976.
  Only the full index was read; no original inspected. She remains unattached.

### Query correction and town variants

The form verified that **fnmo.1 means exact first-name matching**. Earlier descriptions calling it flexible were
incorrect and are corrected below. Removing that flag enables flexible first names; the resulting broad town-wide
candidate rankings are not positive identity matches. Exact town screens returned Kahan/Giulești 17, Kahan/Breb 11,
Kahan/Gyulafalva 1, Moldovan/Giulești 2; Kahan/Bréb, Gyulafalu and Maragyulafalu returned zero under their respective
exact-town queries. The difference between Breb and Bréb demonstrates why one spelling's zero cannot establish
absence. Berbești entries under Comuna Giulești also cannot automatically be treated as births in Giulești village.

### Exact morning per-person screens

Each link records page 1 only, a flexible year where provided, and the town predicate encoded in the URL.
Exact first-name mode is identified separately; candidates still need relationship and original-source checks.
No indexed match under one query does not establish that no birth, marriage or death registration exists.

## Magyar-Zsidó Oklevéltár VII: early Gyulafalva and Bréb evidence — 16 October 2026

User-supplied source, investigated separately from the MyHeritage-only pass. Bibliography verified on the title page:
Fülöp Grünvald and Sándor Scheiber, eds., *Magyar-Zsidó Oklevéltár*, VII, *Az első országos zsidó conscriptiók,
1725–1748* (Budapest, 1963). The scanned **published transcription** was visually inspected; these are not photographs
of the eighteenth-century archival manuscripts. Printed page numbers differ from the PDF viewer numbers.

| Printed page | PDF viewer page | Relevant section and reading |
| --- | --- | --- |
| [136](https://real-eod.mtak.hu/2973/1/Magyar_zsido_okleveltar_VII.pdf#page=138) | 138 | Part of document 15, Máramaros census submitted at Sziget on **8 January 1728** (heading on printed p.134). Under **Gyulafalva**, the leaseholder column records **Moska Nifre**; **Joszip Isak** is entered in the sons column, count 1. No wife is entered in this local block. |
| [306](https://real-eod.mtak.hu/2973/1/Magyar_zsido_okleveltar_VII.pdf#page=308) | 308 | Document 15, **1735 Máramaros census**. The place is printed **Gullfalva**. Three named entries: **Salamon Hirschell**, **Forkos Berke**, **Jacob Iszák**. They are counted as 8, 8 and 4 persons respectively. The place index, printed p.855, explicitly groups Gyulafalva and Gullfalva; this confirms the place, not a family identity. |
| [747](https://real-eod.mtak.hu/2973/1/Magyar_zsido_okleveltar_VII.pdf#page=748) | 748 | Document 10, census dated **4 November 1746, Mármarossziget**. In the Kaszó district: **Brééb** has 1 male head, 1 spouse/widow, no counted children or servants; **Gyulafalva** has 1 male head, 1 spouse/widow, 1 child and 2 servants. No individual names appear in these two rows. |

### Translation and interpretation

The 1728 Gyulafalva prose reports two copper vessels and trade in wine and distilled spirits. The household lived
in nobleman **Stephan Rednik's** house, without land, and had been there three years. Annual house rent was
10 Hungarian florins; the stated annual palatinal levy was 4 Rhenish florins. Residence from about **1725** is
arithmetic from the reported three years, not a birthplace or arrival record. The Joszip entry is in a son column;
its two name elements should not automatically be parsed as a modern given name and inherited surname.

For 1735, the columns distinguish wives, older children, younger children, a tutor, male servants and female servants.
Salamon's row: 1 wife, 1 older child, 4 younger children, 1 male servant; Forkos: 1 wife, 1 older child, 4 younger
children, 1 tutor; Jacob: 1 wife, 2 younger children. Totals include the named household head. The continuation on
printed p.307 supplies livestock, rent and landlord columns; its figures must be aligned across the spread.

For 1746, the column combines spouses **and widows**. The Brééb total is 2 counted persons, assessed at 4 Rhenish
florins; Gyulafalva has 5, assessed at 10. These are counts within this census's categories, not named pedigrees or
proof that only one Jewish family ever lived in each village. The header assesses the listed people, including
children and servants, at 2 Rhenish florins per person. Do not read the final money column as another population count.

### Genealogical consequence

This provides early local names and evidence of Jewish residence in the family's later towns. It **does not establish
parentage for Jozsef, Chaim Arye or Dov Ber**. The 1728 Joszip was already recorded around 62 years before the tree's
estimated birth of Jozsef (c.1790); no identity or descent link is supplied. Similarity between Berke and Burek is also
insufficient. No early individual was attached as an ancestor, and no missing wife was inferred from an empty column.

Next discriminating work: seek named later households and explicit
relationships bridging these eighteenth-century residents to the nineteenth-century Bréb family. Published archival
references: 1728 document ends on p.140 with **O. L. C 29. No 1. 1728-3**; 1735 citation is on p.308 (Acta Judaeorum,
9 September 1735); 1746 document ends on p.748 with **Htt. Acta Jud. f. 992. (304–309)**. These are the edition's
historical citations; current archival call numbers and manuscript images have not been verified. No request was sent.


### Readable English translation of the three pages

The complete checked translation, with editable tables: [read online](magyar-zsido-okleveltar-vii-selected-en.html) or [download Word](magyar-zsido-okleveltar-vii-selected-en.docx). It includes all visible text/rows on printed pp.136, 306 and 747, not only the family-relevant excerpts. The complete 873-PDF-page OCR-based draft is now available; it is not fully image-verified.


The [full-volume English draft](https://avihil1.github.io/kahan-family-tree/research/magyar-zsido-okleveltar-vii-volume-en.html) now covers all 873 PDF pages using Claude Opus. Pages without usable OCR are marked as needing scan inspection. It is translated from OCR and is not fully checked against the images; use the separately reviewed three-page translation for the requested census pages.


## Sefer Marmarosh: identified family passage and 1830 Bréb candidates — 16 October 2026

Source inspected: S. Y. Gross and Y. Yosef Kohen, eds., *Sefer Marmarosh* (Tel Aviv, 1983),
[Yiddish Book Center / NYPL copy](https://www.yiddishbookcenter.org/collections/yizkor-books/yzk-nybc314151/gross-s-y-kohen-yitshak-sefer-marmarosh-meah-ve-shishim-kehilot-kedoshot-be-yishuvan).
Scan identifier **nybc314151**. These are retrospective community histories, not civil registers.
The user's screenshot is the end of **Giulești**, printed **p.63**, above the next community heading, Sarasau.
Reading across the two columns incorrectly can assign Giulești's Holocaust account to Sarasau.
The Hungarian VII citation in that screenshot documents early community context; the family passage is on the preceding page.

### Family evidence: printed pp.62–63

[Printed p.62, PDF viewer p.222, left column beneath the portrait](https://archive.org/details/nybc314151/page/n221/mode/1up); continuation on
[p.63, PDF viewer p.223, upper right](https://archive.org/details/nybc314151/page/n222/mode/1up). Enlarged scans were inspected, not just OCR.

| Person / question | Book report | Assessment against the existing tree |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Chaim Leib Kahana | Timișoara dayan; Rabbi Miller of Nazareth Illit's father-in-law; eldest son. | Very strong identification with Chaim Arye Leb (1903), using the combined family, office and place links. His occupation and Miller connection were already known. |
| Mendel Kahana | Another son; Giulești teacher, later teacher at a religious high school in Netanya. | Strong corroboration of the brother relationship to Shimon Menachem, already identified by family and testimony. The teaching details are newly sourced here. |
| Father | Printed **David Kahana**, village clerk/secretary; five sons and five daughters. | Given name conflicts with original Bernad/Dov evidence. Retain Dov/Bernat; do not equate David and Dov as interchangeable names. The occupation is a book claim for this likely household. |
| Sons' education | All five sons studied with Yehuda Rosner at Székelyhíd. | Chaim's teacher was already known; collective sibling attendance is newly documented, not independently verified in school records. |
| Mother | Unnamed. | Does not independently name Regina Liberman or establish maternal ancestry. |
| Chaim's death | **2 Sivan 5717**. | Conflicts with the present tree's **23 May 1956**. No vital field overwritten; obtain a grave or death original. |
| Mendel's death | Tevet 5733; civil date **1 January 1973**. | Existing Netanya screenshot explicitly dates burial **2 January 1973** and gives death in Hebrew. A Hebrew day starts the previous evening, so this need not be a one-day factual contradiction. Time of death remains unverified; do not call the burial date an independently explicit Gregorian death date. |

The current branch has **11 named children (4 male, 7 female, including an infant death)**; the book says **5 sons,
5 daughters**. These are unreconciled counts. No unnamed fifth son was invented and no daughter deleted.
This source strengthens identification of the known sibling group; it supplies **no link from Dov/Bernat to Chaim
Arye born c.1827 and no mother for Dov**. That ancestral relationship remains provisional.

The village article's bibliography names only an interview with an unidentified former Giulești resident plus
*Magyar-Zsidó Oklevéltár* VII, pp.136, 306, 747. The latter eighteenth-century records cannot support the twentieth-century
family paragraph. The interview itself has not been located; the memoir should not be counted as an original birth record.

### Earlier Bréb lead: printed p.66

[Printed p.66, PDF viewer p.226](https://archive.org/details/nybc314151/page/n225/mode/1up), lower right, lists these household heads under **1830**:
**Moldawan Shlomo — 6 persons; Moldawan Yosif — 5; Moldawan Hers — 6**. The counts are household totals,
not numbers of children. The user-supplied image explicitly labels the counts **כוללים כל הנפשות** (including all persons). Thus the entry does not establish five children; their number is unknown. The Yosif entry is a plausible candidate for our Jozsef, and the other two fit existing
collateral research questions. No ages, parentage or named household members are supplied, so identification is not established.

The same page names **Yechiel Michel Moldawan** and **David Tzvi Cohen** among residents in the 1880s.
They remain unattached candidates. Tzvi/Hers naming equivalence alone does not identify David Hers of the tree,
whose estimated birthplace/residence also needs comparison.

Reliability check: p.66 describes the 1746 Bréb resident as without wife, whereas the inspected original published
census table in VII, printed p.747, has **one head and one spouse/widow**. This concrete disagreement reinforces the
need to resolve the family names/dates in originals. No other book claim was silently promoted to a vital fact.

Next sources: original **1830 Bréb census** naming Moldawan Yosif (household total 5), Shlomo (6), Hers (6);
the interview behind Giulești pp.62–63; Chaim's death/grave original. Bréb's bibliography also cites
Y. Y. Greenwald, *Matzevat Kodesh*, I, New York 1952, **p.22**, and *Oklevéltár* XVI (1976), **p.101**.
The attribution of each individual 1830 item to an original call number is not supplied here. No request was sent.

### Search coverage and limits

HebrewBooks queries: כהנא, מולדובאן, ברעב, יולישט/יולשט; exact גיולשט did not match its index.
Fifty-two HebrewBooks page PDFs were saved before the page-feed returned HTTP 429; a subsequent response supplied
Retry-After 3513 seconds. Further page-feed downloads stopped. An independently available **Yiddish Book Center/NYPL
scan** linked by its catalogue was used instead; its full OCR was screened and relevant printed pages inspected.
The book editions/scans have different viewer pagination; references above use the verified 1983 scan, not guessed
HebrewBooks offsets. This is not an exhaustive identity resolution for every Kahana in the volume. The earlier
Hungarian-volume OCR name search returned no clear Kahan/Moldovan match; poor OCR does not establish absence.


## Hungarian volume VII: family-evidence follow-up — 16 October 2026

This pass concerns the **Hungarian volume itself**, rather than the later Hebrew community history.

**Additional original-document route found:** [plate V, PDF page 870](https://real-eod.mtak.hu/2973/1/Magyar_zsido_okleveltar_VII.pdf#page=870)
reproduces the handwritten **1735 Máramaros census**. The scan was visually inspected. Its Gullfalva brace covers
**Salamon Hirschell, Forkos Berke and Jacob Iszák**, corresponding to the edition's printed pp.306–307.
This is a reproduction of the underlying document, whereas the three cited main-text pages are typeset transcriptions.
The reproduction and transcription derive from the same census and are not independent genealogical witnesses.
Neither gives a relationship to the nineteenth-century Moldovan/Katz/Kahan family.

**Geography resolved:** [printed p.855, PDF page 856](https://real-eod.mtak.hu/2973/1/Magyar_zsido_okleveltar_VII.pdf#page=856)
explicitly indexes **Gyulafalva, Gullfalva: 28, 35, 46**; **Brééb: 46**; **Alsó Róna: 46**;
and **Felső Róna: 28, 35**. The index introduction, printed p.853/PDF854, explains that these numbers are **years**
(1728, 1735, 1746), not page numbers. The index therefore distinguishes Lower Róna from Upper Róna.
The **Lazar** listed at **Fölsö Rhona** in the 1735 table (printed p.308/PDF310) is not the same record as the
**1795 Alsó Rhona Lazar Moldovan** candidate. No identity between those men is established; the earlier row supplies
no Moldovan surname or named parentage connecting them.

**What this contributes to the family question:** named early Gyulafalva households and an identifiable original
census reproduction, but **no established ancestor or additional family relationship**. The 1728 Joszip Isak predates
our Jozsef's estimated birth (c.1790); a repeated name cannot bridge that gap. The 1746 Brééb household remains unnamed.
No new ancestor was attached to the tree from these entries.

Coverage: searched the source OCR and all 873 English-draft page files for Moldav-/Moldov-/Moldaw-, Kahan/Kahana/Katz,
Breb/Brééb and Gyulafalva/Gullfalva; read the three relevant census blocks and their continuations, the index explanation
and Máramaros index, and inspected plate V. No clear Kahan/Katz/Moldovan identity emerged from those searches.
This is **not proof that those names are absent from every image**: OCR and draft table alignment contain errors,
and the full volume has not received a line-by-line image audit. No additional mother or father is identified.
The useful next bridge remains named late-eighteenth-/early-nineteenth-century records for the known Bréb family.


## Scheduled continuation — 17 October 2026

**Search access reset, then the maximum daily limit returned at Moshe Yitzhak Weisberg (baseline index 43,
stable tree key moshe-weisberg). That search did not run. Stop here; no limit bypass or further schedule was attempted.**
The signed-in existing browser was used. Scope: MyHeritage and references actually followed from its records.
This continuation saved 66 query-page captures (one blocked), in addition to the initial successful access check.
There were targeted follow-up searches/context checks for **41 baseline people, indices 2–42**, plus a candidate
and spouse checks. This is a bounded pass, not 41 completed investigations. Initial-screen coverage remains
**86/138**; the 52 people at indices 86–137 still lack a successful initial screen. Named tree nodes remain **138**.

### New evidence and its limits

- **Moses Laser:** [1848 Mojse record, collection 20262, item 98414-](https://www.myheritage.co.il/research/collection-20262/hungary-jewish-census-1848-from-jewishgen?itemId=98414-&action=showRecord) reproduces the already-known age-1 child,
  film 719825, individual 3, with Leb and an unresolved wife indexed as **Ne Afa**. This is a derivative JewishGen index,
  not new proof identifying the later adult; Ne Afa was not adopted as a mother's personal name. A new
  [1906 Baden marriage candidate, collection 10952, item 207769-](https://www.myheritage.co.il/research/collection-10952/austria-vienna-jewish-vital-records-1835-1938?itemId=207769-&action=showRecord), Dr. Moses Laser Kahane / Anna Deutscher,
  file 1906, entry 13, is **index-only**, without identifying ages or parents in the displayed record, and remains unattached.
- **Passenger ambiguity resolved:** [Moses Kahan, collection 10512, item 25489665-](https://www.myheritage.co.il/research/collection-10512/ellis-island-other-new-york-passenger-lists-1820-1957?itemId=25489665-&action=showRecord), Pennsylvania, arrival
  13 July 1905, line 7. The linked original was inspected: NARA T715, roll 599; image group 005263015, image 00735
  (MyHeritage viewer 1053/1120). Moses 50, Anna 44 and Jakob 10 are travelling to Philadelphia; the family-level
  annotation describes Bernet as son/brother. It does **not** establish Moses and our Bernat as brothers.
  The index gives Russian nationality and last residence Kurotki. No family identity was established.
- **Freida Ganz Reif:** [SSA application index, 10863/47742331-](https://www.myheritage.co.il/research/collection-10863/us-social-security-applications-claims-1936-2007?itemId=47742331-&action=showRecord) matches **31 August 1911** and names mother
  **Freida Kahan**; application **14 October 1993**, U.S. citizenship reported, replacement-card application.
  A [linked claim index, 10863/92642826-](https://www.myheritage.co.il/research/collection-10863/us-social-security-applications-claims-1936-2007?itemId=92642826-&action=showRecord) records filing **11 October 1973**, claim **18 October 1973**,
  Brooklyn field office. The [linked SSDI, 10002/9022284-](https://www.myheritage.co.il/research/collection-10002/us-social-security-death-index-ssdi?itemId=9022284-&action=showRecord) matches the known **16 April 1998** death and Brooklyn
  last residence. Combined identity is strong. Dates and mother were already in the tree; newly located government-derived
  evidence corroborates them and adds the claim/application chronology. **Birthplace conflict:** the application index
  says Valea Lungă, Alba, Romania, whereas the baseline says Gyulafalu. No original application was supplied and birthplace
  was not overwritten. The SSA entries are related administrative records, not three independent parentage witnesses.
- **Srul Jankel / Yaakov Ganz:** [MyHeritage BillionGraves index, 10147/86145356-](https://www.myheritage.co.il/research/collection-10147/billiongraves?itemId=86145356-&action=showRecord) led to the
  [photographed original headstone, record 20557023, photo 14560429](https://billiongraves.com/grave/יעקב-גנץ/20557023). It explicitly reads **יעקב בן פרימיד ודוד**,
  with **1922–2004** and **28 Elul 5764**. This independently corroborates the existing parents Frimet and David Ganz;
  the name/date/spouse context strongly identifies the baseline person. The Morasha cemetery location is newly sourced.
  This is **David Ganz**, not Dov/Bernat Kahan of the older branch. No new ancestral generation was inferred.
- **Fani's husband Israel Segelstein:** [JOWBR index, 11019/1981557-](https://www.myheritage.co.il/research/collection-11019/worldwide-jewish-burial-registry-from-jewishgen?itemId=1981557-&action=showRecord) explicitly names wife **Fani Gantz**, age 35,
  Hebrew name **Yisroel ben Shlomo Halevi**, Montreal, Line 1 Grave 11. Its 5 January 1958 date is cautioned as possibly
  burial. The [separate Quebec civil death index, 20872/1327269-](https://www.myheritage.co.il/research/collection-20872/canada-quebec-civil-deaths-1926-1997?itemId=1327269-&action=showRecord) records **death 5 January 1958, Montreal**, born
  1922. Together these support replacing the spouse's vague death-before-2014 label with this date, marked as index evidence.
  **Conflict:** civil marital status is **Divorcé**; the later obituary calls Fani wife of the late Israel. No divorce,
  remarriage or additional spouse is inferred. An original death/marriage record is needed to resolve that field.
- **Pepi/Perle candidate:** [IGRA index 20862/197620-](https://www.myheritage.co.il/research/collection-20862/israel-genealogy-research-association-igra?itemId=197620-&action=showRecord), source **IGRA-1940-ProhibImmig-641**, and
  [20862/2432698-](https://www.myheritage.co.il/research/collection-20862/israel-genealogy-research-association-igra?itemId=2432698-&action=showRecord), **IGRA-1939-deportation-5106**, name Perle Kahan. The first linked IGRA page shows **1940**,
  but identifying details require separate IGRA membership/login. No membership, login or access bypass was attempted.
  No age or parents link these entries to Pepi born 1898. Retain only as an unattached lead.

### Existing knowledge, copied profiles, and unplaced names

The earlier-wife question and Ruchel/Smil 1901-26 remain **prior evidence**, not discoveries of this run.
[MyHeritage's Fani obituary](https://www.myheritage.co.il/research/collection-10679/canadian-obituaries-1997-2017?itemId=1465087-&action=showRecord) and [Rosa obituary](https://www.myheritage.co.il/research/collection-10679/canadian-obituaries-1997-2017?itemId=1465922-&action=showRecord) reproduce the already-known 2014 Gazette notices;
these are alternate access routes to the same sources. Fani's notice names Brahm, Sandra, Jay, Ava and Khloe among
later family generations, but does not identify the intermediate parents under Betty or David; Sandra's exact role
alongside Brahm also needs clarification. No parent-child links were invented. Rosa's notice confirms the already-known
Dori/Leib Tessler pair and deceased son Moishe. A Geni import's Moishe death year **1991** remains an unverified claim.

Frida Weisberg member-tree search results repeat parents Moses Yitzhak / Esther and list **Alte Feige Kahan** among
siblings (e.g. collection 1, item **OYYV6QDBYN2PWLJZRAY6ZLPPAZDASDY-1-500028**). This is a **candidate additional daughter**
of Mozses Iczik, not an established addition: only search-result member-tree claims were inspected; no original or
independent identifying source was found. Some copies duplicate Chaim Leb several times, so sibling counts are unreliable.
A birthplace/year match alone did not attach the candidate. Separate Frida cemetery searching returned other identities.

### Search corrections and interpretation

New scope after yesterday: single-name variants, Moldovan versus Kahan, married surnames, exact historical/modern towns,
collection-specific cemetery searching, and Froim pagination. **Froim Kahan, birth-year relevance 1869: all three pages
(1–20, 21–40, 41–45 of 45) were screened**; page 1 was recaptured because the earlier capture loaded only four cards.
Ephraim (735) and Efraim (187) variants were screened on page 1 only; their remaining pagination is open. A blank early
Efrajm capture is not a negative. Birth years in these queries are search criteria/relevance inputs, not proof that every
returned person falls within that year. The website explicitly broadens unmatched exact first names; its recurrent
Mozes survivor suggestion is not a match for Berl, Volf or other targets.

The old query generator incorrectly used **2014 as a birth year for Moishe Lebovici and Dori Ganz**, whose birth years
are unknown; 2014 came from an obituary context. Today's searches omit birth years for those people and use Dori's
married surname Tessler. Preliminary Fradl 1878 / Salamon Juda 1880 query inputs were corrected to baseline **1879 / 1881**;
both preliminary and corrected captures remain logged. Do not reuse the mistaken inputs.

### Resume frontier

DFS stack: Jozsef → Chaim Arye (1827) → David Hers (c.1855) → Mozses Iczik (1887) → Frida Weisberg →
**Moshe Yitzhak Weisberg (1956), index 43, next query blocked**. Then his recorded children Keren, Roi and Hila,
then Gizi, Jozsef, Rifka, Seindel, and the remaining baseline branches in stored DFS order. Earlier Moses and Efrajm
branches have scoped-pass notes, with adult identity, wider variants and original-record gaps still open.
No high-certainty connection from Dov/Bernat to the 1827 father or from Jozsef to an earlier generation was established.
No new schedule was created; further search requires a later authorized continuation after the limit resets.

## MyHeritage evening continuation — 17 October 2026

Routine searches now use the observed GraphQL API within the existing signed-in session. 89 query captures for
78 baseline people include first queries for the 52 previously unqueried names. All 138 baseline people now have
an initial query capture; the detailed DFS and pagination remain incomplete. No new named descendant was proved.

The strongest new source is [Mozes Junger's original retrospective death registration](https://www.myheritage.co.il/research/collection-11007/germany-hesse-deaths?itemId=2762220-&action=showRecord), HStAMR Best. 926
Nr. 393, Sterbenebenregister 1956, S. 51, entry 5244, Arolsen 4 July 1956 (image 45/400). His exact birth date
17 December 1896 and Máragyulafalva match the tree. The original records death **24 February 1945, Mauthausen**;
MyHeritage indexes March incorrectly. Blank parental/spouse fields leave identification probable. The tree labels it so.

[Eszter Junger née Moldovan](https://www.myheritage.co.il/research/collection-40000/geni-world-family-tree?itemId=811042375&action=showRecord), born 25 November 1876 at Bréb, has the same father/date/town as our Eszter,
but names mother Chaya Feiga Markovits rather than Reize, and marriage to Abraham Junger on 22 June 1896.
This remains a conflicting member-tree candidate: no mother, spouse or descendants merged. Seindel's copied profile
labels the baseline birth date as death; no date was changed. Lipot's Quebec civil index adds corroboration and residence,
while the memorial and Chaja obituary repeat known sources. [Full bilingual per-person ledger](myheritage-dfs.md).


## Mozes Junger: Shoah victim classification — 18 October 2026

Mozes Junger is now explicitly identified as a **Shoah victim**, having perished at **Mauthausen concentration camp on 24 February 1945**, according to the [inspected original retrospective death registration](https://www.myheritage.co.il/research/collection-11007/germany-hesse-deaths?itemId=2762220-&action=showRecord), HStAMR Best. 926 Nr. 393, Sterbenebenregister 1956, S. 51, entry 5244. The identification with this family profile remains **probable**, based on the matching exact birth date and town; the certificate leaves parental and spouse fields blank. This clarifies the historical context of the already documented death. It is not a newly located record or a change in the identity confidence. The tree states this in English and Hebrew.


## Ten additional Hungarian document volumes — 18 October 2026

**Result: new Bréb/Máramaros community evidence, but no new proved ancestor, parentage or descendant.**
The ten new local PDFs were searched across **5,350 PDF pages**. Volume VII, already translated and researched,
was retained as a comparison, bringing the local inventory to 11 volumes / 6,223 PDF pages. These are publication
years from 1965–1980, not successive nineteenth-century civil registers: the new volumes' main document ranges
end between 1741 and 1789. Most precede Jozsef's estimated c.1790 birth. Some editorial material is later.
The [institutional collection catalogue](https://kisebbsegkutato.tk.elte.hu/adatbazis/magyar-zsido-okleveltar) supplies the public PDFs. The books publish documentary
transcriptions; inspecting a scanned book page is not inspecting the original archival manuscript.

### New local entry: Bréb, 1768

**Magyar-Zsidó Oklevéltár XVI (Budapest, title-date 1974), document 22, printed pp. 100–103;
Bréb row in section 22/2, printed p. 101, [PDF page 103](https://kisebbsegkutato.tk.elte.hu/uploads/files/olvasoszoba/magyarzsidookleveltar/Magyar_zsido_okleveltar_XVI.pdf#page=103).**
The introduction on printed p. 100 / PDF 102 dates the county submission **3 June 1768, Máramarossziget**.
Archive citation exactly as published: **O. L. C.-29. Lad. B. fasc. 13. nr. 3/382–3.**
The Bréb row and headings were visually checked in the supplied scan.

| Published field | Transcription/value | English reading |
|---|---|---|
| Settlement | Bréb | Bréb |
| Household name | Moska Moszkovics | Preserve the published name; no identity equation with Moldovan/Kahan |
| Persons | 7 | Headcount including wife and children; **not seven children** |
| Contribution, florins / kreuzer | 3 / — | Contribution amount; dash retained |
| Tolerance tax, florins / kreuzer | 1 / — | Tolerance-tax amount; dash retained |
| Lease/rent, florins / kreuzer | 7 / — | Lease/rent amount; dash retained |
| Occupation | Collective distillers label in the table | Spirits distilling; the printed column applies collectively |

The headcount heading includes **“Capita inclusis uxore et prolibus”**: persons including wife and children.
Wife and children are not named; neither are ages, a father or birthplace. The row cannot tell us seven children's
names, identify Moska as Jozsef's father, or prove continuity with the unnamed 1746 Bréb household previously
found in VII. No person or relationship was added from it. It is linked in Jozsef's profile as **earlier community
context only**. A similar personal name, local residence or plausible generation is insufficient for parentage.

### 1749: Polish origins claimed by the community petitioners

**Volume XIII (Budapest, 1970), documents 206–207, printed pp. 223–224,
[PDF pages 225–226](https://kisebbsegkutato.tk.elte.hu/uploads/files/olvasoszoba/magyarzsidookleveltar/Magyar_zsido_okleveltar_XIII.pdf#page=225), 2 September 1749.** Both printed page images were inspected.
Document 206 describes **96 households**, mainly living by spirits distilling near the Polish border, paying
village contributions, rent and a collective annual protection payment of **100 florins** to the county head.
It is signed by community delegates **Jonas Samson** and **Isaac Samuel**. Archive citation:
**OL. C.-29. Lad. A. fasc. 3. nr. 3. (168.)**

Document 207 asks for relief from **700 Rhenish florins** of tolerance tax. The petitioners state they may be
forced to leave the county and return to Poland, from which they say they had all come. Selected English
translation of the relevant clause: *We would be compelled to leave the county entirely and return to Poland,
from which each and every one of us came, withdrawing ourselves from this unbearable burden.*
Archive citation: **OL. C.-29. Lad. A. fasc. 3. nr. 3. (169–70.)**

This is a collective representation in a petition. It does **not** identify Jozsef or establish Poland as the
birthplace of every later Bréb resident, and the signatories were not attached as relatives. It offers a possible
regional migration background to test against identifying records, not a new origin assigned to your family.

### Coverage and rejected lookalikes

The existing OCR was extracted using `pdftotext -layout`, retaining PDF page boundaries. Searches covered the
complete extracted text, with accent-insensitive variants and inspection of relevant name/place index hits.
Scope included Bréb/Brééb/Breb, Gyulafalva/Gullfalva/Gyulafalu and Giulești, Máramaros/Mármaros/Maromoros/
Marmoros/Marmatia/Sziget, Moldovan/Moldav/Moldaw/Moldow, Kahan/Kahane/Kahana/Kohan/Kohn/Cohn/Cohen/Katz/Catz/Kacz,
and Burek/Burech/Burok variants. Common given names alone were not accepted as identifiers.

False positives include **Moldova/Moldovia as a place**, modern editor/historian **Sámuel Kohn**, the **Katzendorfer
vineyard**, and OCR/name matches in distant communities. Volume XVI's **Simon Kohan** (PDF 28) and **Kohan Salamon**
(PDF 30) are in **Arad County's 1767 returns**, not Máramaros; **Burok Abeles** (PDF 161) is in the Óbuda/Pest
context. No kinship was inferred. Documents about taxes, trade restrictions or a county's petition are not a
register of this family's children.

| Volume | Publication year | Main document dates | PDF pages searched | Result |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [VIII](https://kisebbsegkutato.tk.elte.hu/uploads/files/olvasoszoba/magyarzsidookleveltar/Magyar_zsido_okleveltar_VIII.pdf) | 1965 | 1264–1760 | 545 | Máramaros 1728–1730 robbery petitions; Moldovia is a place, not a Moldovan family identification. |
| [X](https://kisebbsegkutato.tk.elte.hu/uploads/files/olvasoszoba/magyarzsidookleveltar/Magyar_zsido_okleveltar_X.pdf) | 1967 | 1150–1766 | 626 | Surname-like/editorial hits do not identify this family; Gabriel Katz/Rechnitz is a different rabbinic context. |

### Limits and the next original

This is a full-volume OCR/name-and-place search with selected image verification, not a complete translation or
visual reading of every page. Unindexed/image-only pages and OCR errors can conceal names. A no-match outcome
means no identifiable match was established by this method; it does not prove absence from the surviving archives.
Printed page numbers and PDF page numbers are deliberately given separately. No new genealogical edge was accepted.

Prepared request, **not sent**: To the Hungarian National Archives, request the original 1768 Máramaros county
census submitted 3 June 1768, former O. L. C.-29, Lad. B, fasc. 13, nr. 3/382–3, Kaszó district, Bréb row
Moska Moszkovics (seven persons), published in volume XVI, document 22/2, p. 101. Ask for the full sheet, headings,
neighboring rows and any continuation or marginal annotations. The published table does not name wife/children;
the original may or may not add identifying detail. This request does not replace the already prepared Bréb
Register 1271/1838-widow requests, which remain closer to Jozsef's lifetime. No correspondence or fees authorized here.


Verification note: an optional Claude Opus image-review attempt timed out after 300 seconds without a usable review. No Claude confirmation is claimed. The key volume XVI pages 100–101 (PDF 102–103) and volume XIII pages 223–224 (PDF 225–226) were directly checked against the supplied scans. The search pipeline also recovered the previously known Bréb/Gyulafalva references in volume VII as a positive control.


## Audit of Dov/Bernat’s eleven linked children — 18 October 2026

**Not all eleven are verified to the same standard, and the roster is not proved complete.** The current tree has **four males and seven females**. All eleven sex labels have source support; none is based only on the sound of a given name. Explicit M/F values were rechecked today in WikiTree’s verbatim JewishGen birth-index citations through its public getBio API for Jente, Brana, Hane, Ziszel, Leb and Majer Jakab. These are citations to an index, not fresh original-image verification or independent corroboration of that index. The records had previously been checked in the authenticated regional index. For the other five, the basis is explicit family roles in testimony/memorial sources.

**Unresolved count conflict:** Sefer Marmarosh (1983), printed pp.62–63, says five sons/five daughters for the very likely matching household. Omitting Brana, who died in infancy, leaves **four sons/six daughters**, not five/five. The discrepancy could reflect an incomplete or inaccurate account or an unresolved identity in the reconstructed roster; no specific explanation is established. Do not change Hane or another child to male to make the numbers fit. No sex field or parent-child edge changed in this audit. The next decisive work is original birth/death entries for the index-only cases, followed by a reconciled household roster; family-role evidence does not verify every date or prove there were no additional children.

Strongest directly accessible references: [Leb original birth](leb-1903-birth.pdf), [Zisl original stone](https://kdh.org.il/wp-content/themes/firma-child/assets/images/pic/picmaz/26639.jpg), [Abraham Lieber original testimony](https://assets.yadvashem.org/image/upload/t_f_image/f_auto/q_auto/v1/remote_media/arch1_yadvashem/25021218_207_1709/20.jpg), [Reiza daughter-of-Dov original](https://assets.yadvashem.org/image/upload/t_f_image/f_auto/q_auto/v1/remote_media/arch1_yadvashem/22021838_249_1215/6.jpg), and [Sefer Marmarosh family account](https://archive.org/details/nybc314151/page/n221/mode/1up).


### Dov’s own source list: six civil births and an additional child lead

On 18 October 2026 the complete biography and source list of [WikiTree Kahan-376](https://www.wikitree.com/wiki/Kahan-376#Sources) were retrieved successfully through the public `getBio` API. The ordinary web reader failed; this was an access failure, not missing sources. This changed the scope from checking the eleven linked children’s profiles to checking the father’s own citations and research notes.

The list cites **six different birth entries**, all already represented in this tree. Each explicitly identifies Bernat/Bernath/Bernad Kahan and Rifke/Regina Lieberman/Liberman as parents. These are substantive parentage and sex evidence, not merely member-tree relationships:

| Child and date | Sex in cited index | Exact cited archive reference |
|---|---|---|
| Jente, 7 February 1894 | F | Romanian National Archive, Baia Mare Branch, Book 1101, 37-01 |
| Brana, 20 February 1896 | F | Inventory 1371, Book 51, 1896-34; death 5 January 1898, 1898-10, also F |
| Hane, 26 March 1900 | F | Inventory 1373, Book 34, 1900-67 |
| Ziszel, 24 February 1902 | F | Inventory 1373, Book 35, 1902-45; marriage annotation Joachim Muscal, 19 October 1933 |
| Leb, 10 April 1903 | M | Inventory 1373, Book 35, 1903-64; original birth image previously inspected |
| Majer Jakab, 21 March 1905 | M | Inventory 1373, Book 36, 1905-72; marriage annotation Roza Indig, 27 March 1937 |

The citations identify the database as **JewishGen, Máramaros Vital Records Births/Deaths**, retrieved by the WikiTree contributor on 24 July 2024. Re-reading the citations is not a new search of JewishGen and does not turn an index into an inspected original. The same citation repeated on a child’s profile is one underlying source, not independent corroboration.

**Additional child lead: Yosef Mordechai.** Dov’s research notes attribute this name to Ancestry.com trees but provide no linked tree, date, mother, vital entry or explicit sex field. Record him here as a proposed child requiring identifying evidence; do not add an established parent-child edge or assign a male field solely from the name. This is a newly recorded lead in this audit, not a newly proved son or a resolution of the book’s five-son claim.

The same note calls **Meir Yaakov, born 21 May 1905**, a further child. The existing tree already includes **Majer Jakab, born 21 March 1905**, whose cited birth entry also names Roza Indig in a marriage annotation. Similar names and a two-month date discrepancy make a duplicate/variant a plausible explanation, not proof of two boys. Keep one existing Majer/Meir profile; obtain the Ancestry source and compare the original birth and spouse before either merging a distinct identity or creating a second child. No new child or sex assignment was added from these uncited tree claims.


## Family confirmations and Yosef Mordechai search — 18 October 2026

**Family testimony supplied today:** Hillel confirms **Meir Yaakov = Majer Jakab**, one person, and instructs retaining **21 March 1905** from JewishGen, Máramaros Births, Baia Mare Inventory 1373, Book 36, **1905-72**. The conflicting 21 May 1905 appears only in WikiTree Kahan-376’s note attributed to unspecified Ancestry trees; it is not an equally documented civil date. No duplicate child is created. This settles the name equivalence on family testimony; the separately recorded Geni father-surname discrepancy is not resolved by a date correction.

Hillel also explicitly confirms **Yosef Mordechai**, brother of his grandfather Chaim Arye Leb, **survived the Shoah**, and requests adding him. He is now a named male child under Dov/Bernat, with the relationship and survival attributed to family testimony. His mother has not been separately verified in an original. Birth/death dates, birthplace, wife, descendants and immigration date are not supplied. Unknown fields are left blank. The tree now contains **12 children: five sons and seven daughters**. Excluding infant Brana leaves five sons and six daughters, so the five-sons/five-daughters book count is only partially reconciled. This supersedes the morning’s 11-child roster and candidate-only treatment of Yosef.

### Documentary search and identity controls

- [JewishGen, Veteran Zionist Lists—Names from Sighet](https://www.jewishgen.org/hungary/data/sighet/veteranzionistslistsighet1.htm), transcription by Nachi Keren, May 2001: entries **Kahan Josef Marcu, N** and **Kahan Jozef-Marcu, Nuchem-Hersch, Samuel**. The page describes lists compiled in 1943–45 for immigration assistance, with information supplied by relatives. **N** flags other possibly related names in adjacent entries. It does not establish their relationship. The page does not supply this man’s parents, age, birth date, arrival or survival. He remains an unmerged name candidate; Marcu is a search hypothesis for Mordechai, not an established alias. No correspondence was sent.
- Existing signed-in MyHeritage session, observed UI and in-session GraphQL: Yosef Mordechai Kahan **33 results**, pages 1–2 reviewed (20+13 newspaper-index snippets), no identifying family match. Scope broadened to Josef Marcu Kahan **79**, page 1/20 reviewed; Josef Kahan birth-search year1900 **106**, page 1/20; Mordechai Kahan year1900 **52**, page 1/20. These broad result sets were not exhausted and snippets are not original newspaper verification. Exact-town searches Josef Kahan/Giulesti, Mordechai Kahan/Giulesti, Jozsef Kahan/Maragyulafalu, Jozsef Kahan/Falusugatag, Joseph Kahana/Giulesti returned zero. These are indexed query outcomes, not evidence that no record exists. First API capture failed because the page had not loaded; a longer ordinary-load wait captured the working request and the searches then ran successfully. No limit or login was bypassed.
- Israel State Archives’ [Pinpoint immigrant/immigration-document collection](https://journaliststudio.google.com/pinpoint/search?collection=585158a3cc8e02af), 7,818 documents: exact phrases **כהנא יוסף מרדכי**, **יוסף מרדכי כהנא**, **Kahan Josef** returned no OCR matches. **כהן יוסף מרדכי** returned one file (February1949, 0009vcl, second segment), whose snippet refers to a 1922 Bulgaria household; original not inspected, not assigned. Broad document co-occurrence **יוסף מרדכי + כהנא** returned 38 files and Kahan+Josef27 files; these are document-level co-occurrences, not 38/27 people, and were not all image-reviewed.
- **כהן יוסף מרקו** returned one file: [October1949, GL-15494/5, 000a05v](https://journaliststudio.google.com/pinpoint/document-view?collection=585158a3cc8e02af&docid=f00ae5bbed0a6eb2_585158a3cc8e02af&dapvm=2), **scan122/158, printed p.2, row79**, original visually inspected. Heading: arrival **24 October1949, Kedmah**. The man is **Yosef Cohen**, with **father Marco, mother Leah**, origin **Turkey**; Marco belongs to the parent field, not a second given name. This is not evidence for Yosef Mordechai son of Dov. The linked wife and daughter are not imported.
- Shorter exact phrase **כהנא יוסף** returned two files, both original hits inspected: [March1957, GL-15499/7, 000a35a](https://journaliststudio.google.com/pinpoint/document-view?collection=585158a3cc8e02af&docid=6a5bb124347facbd_585158a3cc8e02af&dapvm=2), **scan22/221, printed p.10, rows545–546**: adult **Nizmah Kahana, 1933**, followed by **Yosef, 1950** in the household, a child, not a son of Dov who died1928. [Haifa August–December1934, GL-15489/8, 0009nf9](https://journaliststudio.google.com/pinpoint/document-view?collection=585158a3cc8e02af&docid=fcd885cf67020266_585158a3cc8e02af&dapvm=2), **scan217/408, rows369–370**: **Rachel Kahana,24**, then **Yosef Kopetsik,16** (surname transliteration approximate). The OCR concatenated adjacent rows. Neither hit is attached.

**Result:** family membership and survival recorded from Hillel; no identified arrival record yet. Next discriminating facts are a civil name used in Israel, approximate arrival period, spouse, residence or burial. These can distinguish the target from many Yosef Cohen/Kahana names. No dates, spouse or children are invented to close the search.

### Yosef Mordechai: family birth estimate — 18 October 2026

Hillel initially said Yosef Mordechai was born no earlier than 1906, then clarified: **his guess is 1906–1910**. Record **born approximately 1906–1910**, attributed to Hillel’s family estimate and explicitly unverified. This is a search priority, not an exact birth date or a hard exclusion rule. No new birth or arrival record was identified by this update. The earlier note that no birth information had been supplied is superseded by this estimated range; other unknown dates remain blank.


### Yosef Mordechai: possible son Dov Cohen — 18 October 2026

Hillel tentatively recalled that Yosef Mordechai **probably had a child named Dov Cohen (דב כהן)**. This is recorded in Yosef’s bilingual information note as a **possible son**, not a verified father-son relationship or an identified new person. No dates, mother, spouse or residence were supplied. Do not merge this lead with Dov Kahana (grandson/other branch) or another Dov Cohen by name alone. Search Cohen/Kohen as well as Kahan/Kahana. Public-web searches for the paired names in Hebrew and English on 18 October did not identify a matching father-son source in the returned results; this was not an exhaustive archive search. An original arrival household, marriage or gravestone naming the father could establish the connection.


Dov Cohen birth estimate, supplied by Hillel on 18 October 2026: most probably 1943, possibly 1942 or 1944. Use approximately 1943 (range 1942–1944), not a verified exact year. His relationship as Yosef Mordechai’s son remains tentative.


**Provisional tree leaf added at Hillel’s explicit request, 18 October 2026:** Dov Cohen now appears under Yosef Mordechai, with the existing `possible` marker and a bilingual note identifying the father-son relationship as tentative. Birth is displayed as c.1943, possibly1942–1944, from Hillel’s estimate. This supersedes the earlier decision to keep him only in Yosef’s note. No spouse, mother, descendants or death date are invented; the leaf is a family hypothesis, not new documentary proof.


Death lower bound supplied by Hillel on 18 October 2026: Yosef Mordechai died no earlier than 1986. Record 1986 or later, inclusive; no exact death year, place or grave has been established. Do not convert this into an exact 1986 death.


## Accuracy review, 18 October 2026

This review covers all **140 named profiles currently stored in the tree**. It checks how claims are presented, contradictions within notes, family connections and source provenance. **It does not establish that all 140 people or all their dates and relationships are verified.** Earlier research entries remain a historical log; this section supersedes conflicting wording in them.

The original Be’er Chaim memorial was inspected directly. Its brother Liber, sisters Feiga/Freida/Alta Yenta, and mother Rivka (wife of Dov Kahana) appear in separate lines. The former quotation “Liber Kahana, child of Dov and Rivka” was a reconstruction incorrectly presented as literal wording. Identification with Abraham Lieber additionally uses his brother’s separate Page of Testimony 1501887. The memorial itself gives no individual vital dates or death places. The title page identifies a deceased author, formerly of Timișoara and later rabbi in Jaffa; his writings were edited by his son-in-law and sons. Bnei Brak, 5748 (1987–1988), is the publication, not the author’s lifespan.

The same memorial names the author’s father-in-law Yitzhak Aizik Miller and mother-in-law Henya, followed by **their daughter Yenta Zefir and son Ezra**. Thus these two are siblings-in-law. Yad Vashem 10991958 and 10991960 assigns them to Chaim Arye and Leah; the inspected original contradicts that indexing. They are not added as the author’s children. This is a source-reading correction, not an additional independent witness.

Exact originals: [title page, scan 00006](https://assets.yadvashem.org/image/upload/t_f_image/f_auto/q_auto/v1/remote_media/documentation4/5/full_pdf_srika/9835770/00006.jpg); [complete memorial, scan 00008](https://assets.yadvashem.org/image/upload/t_f_image/f_auto/q_auto/v1/remote_media/documentation4/5/full_pdf_srika/9835770/00008.jpg). Yad Vashem source collection O.41.3, digitized book folder 9835770. The original birth of Leb, 10 April 1903, remains [the inspected civil entry](https://avihil1.github.io/kahan-family-tree/research/leb-1903-birth.pdf), with [English translation](https://avihil1.github.io/kahan-family-tree/research/leb-1903-birth-en.html).

Other corrections: parental death details are no longer presented as individually verified dates/places for ten named children in the Adler, Wieder and Kahan households. Menachem’s 2 January 1973 is explicitly burial, with Hebrew death date 28 Tevet 5733 and unresolved Gregorian conversion. Jozsef’s c.1790/c.1837 dates are visibly estimates. Dov/Bernat’s 1928 grave remains a proposed identification. Reiza’s and Abraham Lieber’s conflicting birth claims remain visible. Majer Jakob’s 1881 death and retrospective 1902 registration are distinguished. Lajos/Leib is a candidate pairing, not proof of identity. Stale contradictory family notes were reconciled without changing the 140-person topology or sex labels.

Coverage and limit: all 140 existing profile notes and displayed fields were reviewed against the available research ledger and cached citations; that is not 140 new source searches. A fresh WikiTree API pass returned eight biographies and nine profile responses before HTTP 429. Further refreshes stopped; failed requests are not negative findings. Existing citations were used for the remaining people. Yad Vashem structured entries were re-read for the book-derived family and selected child testimonies; derived entries sharing one original were counted once. Uninspected vital registers, unidentified stones and original birth/death records remain outstanding. Family recollections are retained as family testimony; provisional Dov Cohen remains provisional.

Read each row together with that person’s linked sources and current tree note. “Unresolved” means no reliable value established here, not that the event did not occur. This ledger inventories current claims; it is not a certification of every imported date.
